 {"content": "**Repository Summary**\n- **Purpose**: Odoo 19 custom addons to manage a sports federation (clubs, teams, seasons, tournaments, rules, officiating, rosters, results, public site, reporting).\n- **Location**: All addons live under the `odoo/` folder as individual modules prefixed with `sports_federation_`.\n-- **Primary docs**: See [odoo/CONTEXT.md](odoo/CONTEXT.md#L1), [odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md](odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md#L1), and the Workflows in [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md#L1).\n\n**Architecture & Patterns**\n- **Module split**: `sports_federation_base` (master data), `sports_federation_tournament` (tournaments, matches, participants), `sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling` (service/wizards), plus domain modules (`people`, `rules`, `officiating`, `rosters`, `result_control`, `public_site`, `reporting`, `finance_bridge`, `venues`, `discipline`, `standings`, ...).\n- **Model naming**: Domain models use the `federation.` prefix (e.g. `federation.player`, `federation.match`).\n- **Common patterns**: `ir.sequence` for identifiers, `state` selection fields for workflows, wizards for batch operations (under `wizards/`), `controllers/` for portal/website endpoints, and `security/` for groups/record rules/ACLs.\n\n**Key Files & Where To Look**\n- **High-level context**: [odoo/CONTEXT.md](odoo/CONTEXT.md#L1)\n- **Technical architecture**: [odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md](odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md#L1)\n-- **Workflows (authoritative behavioural spec)**: [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md#L1) and the other files inside `odoo/_workflows`.\n- **Module READMEs**: each module may have a `README.md` under `odoo/<module>/README.md` with implementation notes and expectations.\n-- **Install logs / historical notes**: `_logs/` contains installation and setup notes per module.\n\n**Development Guidelines (how to implement changes)**\n- **Add/modify a model**: create/modify `models/*.py`, export package in `models/__init__.py`, add access rules in `security/ir.model.access.csv`, add any initial `data/*.xml` and register them in `__manifest__.py` `data` or `demo` lists, add views in `views/*.xml`, update `menu_items.xml` as needed, and add tests under `tests/`.\n- **Add a wizard or service**: put transient models under `wizards/` and views under `views/` (manifest `data`), keep service logic in modules like `sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling` (no persistent models if not required).\n- **Add a controller / portal flow**: use `controllers/` and add templates under `views/` or `templates/`; respect portal record rules (see `sports_federation_portal`).\n- **Security**: always add or update `security/ir.model.access.csv` and any `security/*.xml` groups/rules for new models or new endpoints.\n- **Manifest hygiene**: update `__manifest__.py` `depends`, `data`, and `version` fields when introducing DB changes or new data files.\n\n**Testing & Local Run**\n- **Where tests live**: `odoo/<module>/tests/` when present (some modules include unit/functional tests).\n- **Run module tests**: use the Odoo test runner for module-level tests (e.g. `odoo-bin -d <db> -i <module> --test-enable`) or your established local development workflow.\n- **Release sanity**: include at least one focused test for new business logic (especially schedule generation, result pipeline, and state transitions).\n\n**PR Checklist (required before merge)**\n- **Tests**: Add/adjust tests covering the new behaviour.\n- **Security**: Update `security/ir.model.access.csv` and add group/record-rule XML if necessary.\n- **Manifest**: Add new data files to `__manifest__.py` `data` list and ensure `depends` are correct.\n-- **Docs**: Update or add a short README in the module and, if behaviour changes, update the relevant `odoo/_workflows` file.\n- **Small, focused commits**: Prefer atomic commits (one logical change per commit) and descriptive commit messages.\n\n**How Copilot Should Help (recommended prompts & behaviour)**\n- **Primary rule**: Prefer conservative, minimal changes that keep module boundaries intact. Avoid touching unrelated modules unless absolutely required and explain why.\n- **When asked to implement a model change**: return a plan and a single patch that includes: the `models/<file>.py` change, `models/__init__.py` export, `security/ir.model.access.csv` entry, `views/*.xml` (if UI), `__manifest__.py` update, and a new test under `tests/` that exercises the main logic.\n- **When asked to add a wizard or generator**: create a transient model in `wizards/`, a wizard view, and a small deterministic example in tests that demonstrates expected output (e.g. round-robin schedule for 4 participants).\n- **When asked about business rules or workflows**: consult and reference the Workflow docs in `odoo/_workflows` and the `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md` to ensure behaviour matches the documented flows.\n\n**Example prompts you can give Copilot in this repo**\n- **Add a model**: \"Add a new persistent model `federation.referee` with fields `name`, `certification_level`, `active`; add ACLs, a basic tree/form view, and unit tests. Show me the patch with files changed.\"\n- **Create a wizard**: \"Create a `round_robin_wizard` that generates matches for a stage; include wizard model, view, manifest update, and a unit test verifying match count for 4 teams.\"\n- **Fix workflow behaviour**: \"Investigate why standings recompute includes contested results; propose a minimal fix and tests to prevent contested results from being counted.\"\n\n**Notes & Expectations**\n- **Authoritative sources**: the `_workflows` documents and `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md` are the source of truth for business behaviour—use them before changing workflows.\n- **Keep modules decoupled**: the architecture intentionally separates `base`, `tournament`, and the `competition_engine` service; prefer adding features in the appropriate module.\n- **Ask clarifying questions**: if a requested feature impacts tournament rules, scheduling, or publication flows, ask which workflow (`_workflows/*.md`) should govern the behaviour.\n\n**Documentation Maintenance (required)**\n\n- **Always update docs**: For any code, data model, view, workflow, or behaviour change, update the relevant documentation files in this repository as part of the same change set (or in a linked follow-up PR). At minimum, consider updating `odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md`, `odoo/CONTEXT.md`, the module-level `README.md` under the affected `odoo/<module>/`, and any affected workflow files under `odoo/_workflows/`.\n- **Doc scope**: Document API/ORM changes, new models, new fields, migration notes (DB changes), public website impacts, and any user-facing behaviour changes. Include brief examples or CLI commands for running tests or sample data when useful.\n- **PR checklist enforcement**: Fail-fast — do not open a PR merging code that introduces behavioural or schema changes without documentation entries. If you cannot edit docs yourself (e.g., lacking domain clarity), leave a clear TODO in the code and notify repository maintainers.\n- **Why**: Keeping documentation current ensures maintainability, reduces onboarding friction, and prevents accidental regressions in workflows that rely on textual contracts (workflows, assumptions, and operational steps).\n\n", "content_length": 7327, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": ".github/copilot-instructions.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "7e616893749fb5ae32de33c88010309516272742120b3b28cec305c140409842", "size_bytes": 7331}

 {"content": "# Sports Federation — Odoo Addons (Odoo 19)\n\nLightweight collection of Odoo 19 addons to manage a sports federation: clubs,\nteams, seasons, tournaments, scheduling, officiating, rosters, results,\nstandings, portal pages and reporting.\n\nThis repository contains modular, opinionated addons named\n`sports_federation_<domain>` (e.g. `sports_federation_tournament`). The code is\ndesigned for clarity: algorithmic logic lives in `services/`, interactive flows\nuse `wizards/`, and persistent objects in `models/`.\n\nTable of contents\n- Architecture overview\n- Quickstart / Installation\n- Development & tests\n- Contributing & docs\n- Module list (high level)\n\nQuick links\n- High-level context: `CONTEXT.md`\n- Technical notes: `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md`\n- Documentation map: `docs/README.md`\n- Architecture decisions: `adr/README.md`\n- Route inventory: `ROUTE_INVENTORY.md`\n- Release runbook: `RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md`\n- Data retention policy: `DATA_RETENTION_POLICY.md`\n- Module owners: `MODULE_OWNERS.yaml`\n- Demo data pack: `sports_federation_demo/README.md`\n- Integration contracts: `INTEGRATION_CONTRACTS.md`\n- Managed integration OpenAPI: `openapi/integration_v1.yaml`\n- Workflows: `_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md`\n- Contributor guide: `CONTRIBUTING.md`\n\n**Architecture overview**\n\nHigh-level architecture (graph):\n\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\n  subgraph Core\n    Base[sports_federation_base]\n    Tournament[sports_federation_tournament]\n    Engine[sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling]\n    Standings[sports_federation_standings]\n  end\n  subgraph Support\n    People[sports_federation_people]\n    Rosters[sports_federation_rosters]\n    Officiating[sports_federation_officiating]\n    Venues[sports_federation_venues]\n    Finance[sports_federation_finance_bridge]\n    Public[sports_federation_public_site]\n  end\n\n  Engine -->|generates| Tournament\n  Engine -->|generates| Matches[federation.match]\n  Matches -->|belong to| Round[federation.tournament.round]\n  Round --> Venues\n  Matches --> Standings\n  Standings -->|feeds| Progression[federation.stage.progression]\n  Progression --> Tournament\n  Matches --> Finance\n  Rosters --> Matches\n  Officiating --> Matches\n  Public --> Standings\n\n  classDef core fill:#f8f9fa,stroke:#333\n  class Base,Tournament,Engine,Standings core;\n```\n\nNotes:\n- The `competition_engine` contains deterministic scheduling services (round-\n  robin, knockout). It supports per-round scheduling, round-owned date/venue\n  planning, and\n  full-bracket construction.\n- `federation.tournament.round` is the shared schedule block for a stage: rounds\n  own the calendar date and venue, while matches keep the exact kickoff time.\n- Standings computation and `stage_progression` rules automate advancement\n  across stages (optional `auto_advance`). See `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md`.\n\nQuickstart / Installation (example)\n\nPrerequisites\n- Python 3.10+ (for local linting and editor tooling)\n- Docker with Compose current (for the containerized Odoo test runner)\n- PostgreSQL 12+ only if you are running against a separate local Odoo checkout\n- Node.js/npm (optional: for asset tooling)\n- wkhtmltopdf (optional: PDF export)\n\nExample (local development on Windows / Powershell):\n\n```powershell\ngit clone REPOSITORY_URL\ncd REPO_ROOT/odoo\npython -m venv .venv\n.\\.venv\\Scripts\\Activate.ps1\npython -m pip install --upgrade pip\npython -m pip install -r requirements.txt\nblack --check sports_federation_base sports_federation_tournament sports_federation_standings sports_federation_venues sports_federation_portal sports_federation_public_site ci\nflake8 sports_federation_base sports_federation_tournament sports_federation_standings sports_federation_venues sports_federation_portal sports_federation_public_site ci\n```\n\nContainerized module tests (Git Bash / WSL on Windows, or any POSIX shell):\n\n```bash\ncp ci/.env.example ci/.env\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_standings\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --suite portal_public_ops\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --suite rosters_readiness_guard\n```\n\nValidate CI helper scripts before pushing changes:\n\n```bash\nbash -n ci/run_tests.sh\nbash -n ci/apply_env_to_ir_config.sh\npython3 ci/check_ci_hygiene.py\nbash ci/prune_ci_logs.sh 30\n```\n\nNotes and tips\n- `requirements.txt` pins repository-local tooling only. The Odoo runtime used by CI comes from the `odoo:19` Docker image declared in `ci/docker-compose.ci.yaml`.\n- Keep local runtime credentials in `ci/.env`; do not commit that file. The checked-in `ci/.env.example` is the safe template.\n- This repository does not ship `odoo-bin`. If you use a separate local Odoo checkout, point its `addons_path` at this repository and run tests from that checkout.\n- Use the module manifest `__manifest__.py` `data` entries to register new\n  views/security/data files. If you add or change models, update\n  `security/ir.model.access.csv` and include migration notes in the docs.\n- To run module tests (example):\n\n```bash\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling\n```\n\nDevelopment & tests\n- Keep changes small and focused. Add at least one unit/integration test for\n  any change that affects scheduling, standings, or progression logic.\n- Where possible put algorithmic code in `services/` and keep models simple.\n- Use `wizards/` for interactive, admin-driven flows (preview + confirm).\n\nContributing & docs\n- Always update repository documentation as part of the same change set.\n  Update `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md`, relevant `_workflows/*`, and the affected module\n  `README.md`.\n- Treat `ROUTE_INVENTORY.md`, `INTEGRATION_CONTRACTS.md`, and `RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md`\n  as maintained release documents, not one-off notes.\n- Follow the PR checklist in `.github/copilot-instructions.md` and add tests\n  for behavioural changes. If you cannot update docs immediately, add a clear\n  TODO in the change and notify maintainers.\n- The canonical lifecycle and ownership reference for the core records is in\n  `STATE_AND_OWNERSHIP_MATRIX.md`.\n- Use `CONTRIBUTING.md` for the maintainer workflow: local prerequisites,\n  env setup, focused CI suites, and pre-push validation commands.\n\nModule list (high level)\n- `sports_federation_base` — master data (clubs, teams, seasons)\n- `sports_federation_tournament` — tournaments, stages, groups, matches\n- `sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling` — scheduling services and wizards\n- `sports_federation_standings` — standings computation and publishing\n- `sports_federation_rosters` — rosters and match-sheets\n- `sports_federation_officiating` — referee registry and assignments\n- `sports_federation_venues` — venues, playing areas, and round-level venue scheduling\n- `sports_federation_finance_bridge` — finance event helpers\n- `sports_federation_public_site` / `sports_federation_portal` — public pages and portal flows\n", "content_length": 6852, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "README.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "9346942c44e8ee70916ab3f47f4b0703ad698e817a9349f65dfde3b4e48bb10e", "size_bytes": 6872}
 {"content": "# Release Runbook\n\nLast updated: 2026-08-23\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nLast reviewed: 2026-08-23\nReview cadence: Every release\nRelease train: 2026.08\n\nThis runbook is the canonical operator checklist for promoting the federation\nstack with repeatable verification, upgrade, and rollback steps.\n\n## Preconditions\n\n1. Confirm the target branch is merged and the working tree is clean enough to\n   identify the intended release diff.\n2. Confirm Docker services are healthy with the production compose file.\n3. Confirm enough disk space exists for both a PostgreSQL dump and a filestore\n   archive under `./backups/`.\n\n## Documentation Freshness\n\nBefore cutting a release, verify that the freshness-tracked docs still match the\nchange set whenever route ownership, architecture, CI policy, or operational\nguidance changed:\n\n```bash\npython3 addons/ci/check_doc_freshness.py\npython3 addons/ci/check_markdown_links.py\npython3 addons/ci/check_module_owners.py\npython3 addons/ci/check_openapi_contracts.py\n```\n\nIf the release changed any tracked surface, update the affected document or\narchive it in the same release branch before proceeding.\n\nIf the release changed portal privilege boundaries, reporting SQL view policy,\nor canonical public route ownership, review the affected record under `adr/`\nand update it in the same release branch when the decision changed.\n\nIf the release includes model, view, or controller ownership changes, confirm\nthe migration-review gate passes and that every affected module has either\nrelease-note coverage or an explicit migration script:\n\n```bash\npython3 addons/ci/check_migration_review.py --base-ref origin/main\n```\n\nFor the same release branch, record migration evidence and rollback notes in\nthis runbook:\n\n```bash\n# add the commands, outcomes, rollback triggers, and SQL/drop steps\n${EDITOR:-vi} addons/RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md\n```\n\n### Portal controller-load repair evidence\n\nThe portal repair removes the stale re-export of the deleted V1 workspace\ncontroller, restores the tournament-registration view in the manifest data\norder, and keeps officiating assignment visibility limited to live current\npublications. No database migration is required; the portal manifest is bumped\nfrom `19.0.4.0.0` to `19.0.4.1.0`.\n\nDry-run evidence captured on 2026-08-23:\n\n```text\n./scripts/upgrade_sports_federation.sh --db odoo --dry-run\nExit code: 0\nUpgrade: sports_federation_base,...,sports_federation_portal,...,sports_federation_venues\nInstall: none\n```\n\nFocused verification:\n\n```text\nbash ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_portal\nExit code: 0; 116 tests run, 116 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors; 30 post-tests\n```\n\nRollback is the standard backup restore procedure in this runbook. Do not\nrestore the deleted V1 controller or broaden portal publication access as a\nrollback workaround.\n\nIf the release changes addon responsibility boundaries or adds a new\n`sports_federation_*` module, update `MODULE_OWNERS.yaml` in the same release\nbranch and rerun the registry check before cutting the release.\n\nIf the release changes log retention, staged-delivery cleanup, or generated\nreport artifact handling, update `DATA_RETENTION_POLICY.md` in the same branch\nand confirm the affected cleanup crons still match the documented windows.\n\n## Pre-Release Verification\n\nRun the focused suites that cover the highest-risk federation workflows:\n\n```bash\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite portal_public_ops\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite finance_reporting\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite release_surfaces\n```\n\nIf the release changed retention jobs or cleanup scope, also re-run the\naffected module suites directly:\n\n```bash\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_notifications\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_import_tools\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_reporting\n```\n\nThese suites now include query-budget regression checks for the public-site,\nportal, and reporting hotspots documented in `TESTING_GUIDE.md`.\n\nIf the release changes the largest reporting SQL views, refresh the committed\n`EXPLAIN` snapshots from a restored or staging database and review the diff in\n`addons/ci/explain_snapshots/` before promoting the release:\n\n```bash\npython3 addons/ci/capture_explain_snapshots.py --db odoo_restore_drill\n```\n\nIf the release changes only one module, also run that module directly before the\nbroader suites:\n\n```bash\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_reporting\n```\n\n## Upgrade Dry Run\n\nPrint the resolved module list and backup target before touching the database:\n\n```bash\n./scripts/upgrade_sports_federation.sh --db odoo --dry-run\n```\n\nIf you need to restrict the release to a subset of modules:\n\n```bash\n./scripts/upgrade_sports_federation.sh --db odoo --modules sports_federation_reporting,sports_federation_portal --dry-run\n```\n\n### Competition ownership change evidence\n\nThe legacy monolithic competition engine is removed from the addon tree. The\nreplacement ownership chain is `sports_federation_competition_core`,\n`sports_federation_registration`, `sports_federation_format`,\n`sports_federation_calendar`, `sports_federation_scheduling`,\n`sports_federation_schedule_approval`, and `sports_federation_matchday`.\n\nBefore upgrading a database that previously installed the legacy engine:\n\n1. Take the database and filestore backups described above.\n2. Run the upgrade script with `--dry-run` and confirm that the resolved module\n   list contains the chain and does not contain the removed addon.\n3. Run the upgrade in a disposable restore of the backup first, then verify\n   registration, format freeze, calendar capacity, scheduling, approval\n   publication, and match-day operations.\n4. Record the dry-run output and restore-drill result with the release ticket;\n   do not silently reinterpret legacy planner or published-schedule records.\n\nIf the restore drill or production upgrade fails, stop Odoo, preserve the\nfailed logs, restore the database and filestore backup, and restart the prior\nrelease. Do not reinstall the removed engine as an ad-hoc rollback; use the\nreviewed database migration or rollback procedure for the target release.\n\n### Legacy ownership-removal review record\n\nThe repository-side review for this ownership change was completed against the\nrecorded base commit before preparing the release patch. The following checks\nare the minimum evidence to attach to the release ticket:\n\n```bash\nbash -n addons/ci/run_tests.sh\nbash -n addons/scripts/ci/run_rc_validation.sh\npython3 addons/ci/check_legacy_engine_removed.py\ngit -C addons apply --check addons/legacy_removal.patch\n```\n\nThe shell checks, loadability guard, and clean-worktree patch check must all\npass. The database restore drill remains mandatory before production upgrade;\nits output must be attached to the release ticket rather than inferred from\nrepository checks. No historical migration directory is reused for this\nownership removal, and no production record is silently rewritten.\n\nRollback is backup-based: stop the target release, preserve logs and the failed\ndatabase state for review, restore the pre-upgrade database and filestore, and\nrestart the previous release. If a data transformation is later required, it\nmust be introduced in a new reviewed migration with an explicit backfill and\nrollback plan; reinstalling the removed addon is not an approved rollback.\n\n## Backups\n\nThe upgrade script performs backups by default. It stores:\n\n- `modules.txt` with the exact install/upgrade module list\n- `<db>_<timestamp>.dump` as a PostgreSQL custom-format dump\n- `filestore_<db>_<timestamp>.tar.gz` when a filestore exists under\n  `./odoo-data/filestore/<db>`\n\nDo not use `--skip-backup` for production releases.\n\nRun the periodic restore drill against one of these backup directories before\nor during each release train using the restore checklist in this runbook:\n\n```bash\nbash addons/ci/restore_backup_drill.sh --backup-dir 2026-04-15_191410 --target-db odoo_restore_drill --dry-run\n```\n\n## Production Upgrade\n\nRun the upgrade and let the script restart the live Odoo service afterward so\nPython changes are loaded by the running web container:\n\n```bash\n./scripts/upgrade_sports_federation.sh --db odoo\n```\n\nFor a non-interactive upgrade that excludes demo data, use `--yes`. To include\nthe demo addon non-interactively, add `--install-demo`.\n\nThe script installs modules that are missing or uninstalled, upgrades modules\nthat are already installed, and restarts the Odoo service. It runs:\n\n- `odoo -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf -d <db> -i <install_module_csv> -u <upgrade_module_csv> --stop-after-init`\n- `docker compose restart odoo`\n\nThe demo addon is excluded by default. Interactive runs ask whether\n`sports_federation_demo` should be installed. Use `--install-demo` when that\nchoice is intentional and the upgrade is non-interactive.\n\n## Post-Upgrade Verification\n\nVerify these operator checkpoints immediately after the upgrade:\n\n1. Open Federation > Reporting > Operator Checklist and confirm there are no\n   unexpected blocked queues.\n2. Open Federation > Reporting > Report Schedules and confirm there are no new\n   `Last Run Failed` schedules.\n3. Open Federation > Import Tools > Inbound Deliveries and confirm there are no\n   unexpected `failed` or `processed_with_errors` deliveries.\n4. Validate the public and portal release surfaces manually if the release\n   touched them:\n   - `/web/login`\n   - `/tournaments`\n   - `/tournaments/<slug>/register`\n   - `/my/teams/new`\n   - `/my/season-registration/new`\n   - `/my/compliance`\n5. Trigger one scheduled report manually from Federation > Reporting > Report\n   Schedules if the release touched reporting code.\n\n## Integration Partner Token Rotation\n\nAfter any release that modifies integration partner credentials, or on a\nscheduled rotation cycle, rotate partner tokens using the following procedure:\n\n**When to rotate:**\n- After upgrading `sports_federation_import_tools` (any release that changed\n  token storage, authentication, or the integration controller surface).\n- When the `token_rotation_required` flag is set to `True` on a partner record\n  (visible in Federation > Import Tools > Integration Partners).\n- On a periodic schedule — at minimum once per year or whenever a partner\n  personnel change occurs.\n\n**How to rotate (back-office procedure):**\n\n1. Open **Federation > Import Tools > Integration Partners**.\n2. Filter for partners where **Token Rotation Required** is checked, or where\n   **Last Rotated On** is older than the rotation policy window.\n3. For each partner, open the form and click **Rotate Token** (requires\n   Federation Manager group). Confirm the dialog.\n4. The wizard reveals the new raw token **once** (it cannot be retrieved again).\n   Copy it immediately and deliver it to the partner over a secure channel\n   (not email in plain text).\n5. The `token_rotation_required` flag clears automatically after a successful\n   rotation.\n\n**After a migration from plaintext storage:**\n\nIf `sports_federation_import_tools` was upgraded from a version prior to\n`19.0.1.2.0`, existing plaintext tokens were hashed in place and flagged for\nrotation by the post-migration script. Partners with `Token Rotation Required`\nset to `True` are still functional (their hashed token verifies correctly)\nbut should be rotated and re-issued at the next opportunity.\n\n**Verification:**\n\nAfter rotation, ask the partner to make one authenticated test call and confirm\na `200 OK` response to `/integration/v1/contracts`. A `401` response with\n`access_denied` indicates the token was not delivered correctly.\n\n## Rollback\n\nIf the upgrade must be rolled back:\n\n1. Stop or scale down the Odoo service to prevent new writes.\n2. Restore the PostgreSQL dump from the relevant backup directory.\n3. Restore the matching filestore archive.\n4. Restart the Odoo service.\n5. Re-run the post-upgrade verification checklist against the restored system.\n\nExample restore outline:\n\n```bash\ndocker compose stop odoo\ndropdb -U odoo odoo\ncreatedb -U odoo odoo\npg_restore -U odoo -d odoo backups/<timestamp>/odoo_<timestamp>.dump\ntar -xzf backups/<timestamp>/filestore_odoo_<timestamp>.tar.gz -C odoo-data/filestore\ndocker compose up -d odoo\n```\n\nAdjust database names and paths to match the selected backup directory.\n\n---\n\n## Upgrade Path Notes (per release train)\n\nThis section records DB migrations, new `ir.config_parameter` keys, deprecated\nfield removals, and module install order changes introduced in each release.\nAdd a subsection here for every release train that makes schema or behavioural\nchanges. Reference the compatibility table in `INTEGRATION_CONTRACTS.md` for\nroute retirement dates.\n\n### Release 2026.05\n\n**DB migrations**: None.\n\n### Release 2026.08\n\n**DB migrations**: None. The roster match-sheet change only suppresses the\ninteractive match creation side effect while Odoo loads demo fixtures; normal\nORM-created matches still receive their draft home and away sheets. The demo\nfixture was also aligned with current participant eligibility and compliance\nexpiry-date validation.\n\n**Validation evidence**: A fresh isolated install with demo data enabled passed\nthe demo module tests (5 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors) and did not report the\n`federation_match_sheet_unique_match_team_side` violation.\n\n**Rollback**: Revert the additive code and fixture changes. No database\nbackfill, schema reversal, or migration artifact is required.\n\n## Release train convention\n\nThis runbook and `ROADMAP.md` are the two authoritative release-train\nsurfaces. Use `YYYY.MM` for the active train identifier and update both files\nwhen the operating window changes. Before cutting a new train:\n\n1. Archive or replace superseded roadmap commitments.\n2. Align the `Release train:` metadata in this file and `ROADMAP.md`.\n3. Confirm module release notes and migration evidence use the same train.\n4. Run documentation, migration, workflow, and release checks before promotion.\n\n## Release acceptance checklist\n\n- [ ] Domain-integrity gate is green for every active division.\n- [ ] Workflow, portal ownership, and security certification is green.\n- [ ] Performance, concurrency, and migration checks are green.\n- [ ] Restore and operations verification is green.\n- [ ] Static CI, fresh install, restored-database upgrade, full Odoo tests,\n         frontend tests, and production-like smoke tests are green.\n- [ ] Backup/restore rehearsal, artifact checksum, rollback owner, and rollback\n         trigger are recorded.\n\n## Production gates\n\n- **Domain integrity:** played or published history must not be removed by\n   cascades; stage, gameday, match, participant, and progression records must\n   remain inside their division.\n- **Security:** all cross-club substitution tests must deny access; modifying\n   routes must be authenticated, state-checked, ownership-scoped, and CSRF\n   protected.\n- **Performance and concurrency:** validate migration indexes with\n   `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on anonymized production-like data; preserve\n   revision and domain invariants for slot assignment, publication, deletion,\n   registration, roster editing, and result approval.\n- **Operational health:** run the sanitized operational health snapshot after\n   restore and upgrade, then execute the canonical smoke journeys.\n\n## Backup and restore drill\n\nRun at least once per release train and after backup, compose, or filestore\nlayout changes:\n\n```bash\nbash addons/ci/restore_backup_drill.sh \\\n   --backup-dir <backup-directory> \\\n   --target-db odoo_restore_drill --dry-run\n```\n\nThen run without `--dry-run`. Confirm the dump, `modules.txt`, and optional\nfilestore are restored, every listed module is present, secrets are replaced in\nthe acceptance environment, health/integrity checks pass, and scheduled jobs\nremain disabled until verification completes. Record the date, backup, target\ndatabase, and outcome here or in the release log, then drop the disposable DB.\n\n## Migration evidence and rollback record\n\nFor every migration-sensitive change, record the affected modules, migration\nor ownership surfaces, command output summary, test result, and known warnings\nin the release notes. Record rollback triggers, backup requirements, migration\nartifacts, and any index or data reversal in the same release entry. The\nvalidated 2026.06 tournament migration backfilled missing match schedule and\nround fields and added five operational indexes; its rollback path is restoring\nthe paired database/filestore or dropping those indexes when only index\nrollback is required. The 2026.08 workspace and portal ownership cleanup was\nrollback-neutral.\n\n**New `ir.config_parameter` keys**:\n\n| Key | Purpose | Default |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sports_federation.rate_limit.<scope>.limit` | Per-scope rate-limit ceiling override | See `_POLICIES` in `request_rate_limit.py` |\n| `sports_federation.rate_limit.<scope>.window_seconds` | Per-scope window override | See `_POLICIES` |\n\n**Deprecated field removals**: None.\n\n**Module install order changes**: None. Default install order (tier 1 → 2 → 3 → 4)\nis unchanged; see `DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md`.\n\n**Behaviour changes**:\n\n- Rate-limit policies are now overridable at runtime via `ir.config_parameter`\n  without a code deployment.  {"content": "# Route Inventory\n\nLast updated: 2026-08-20\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nLast reviewed: 2026-08-23\nReview cadence: Every release\n\n## Purpose\n\nThis file inventories critical browser, portal, public, reporting, and managed-integration entry points. It is not intended to duplicate every controller decorator. A route belongs here when it is public, partner-facing, privileged, contract-versioned, or operationally important.\n\nThe machine-readable route inventory and controller tests remain the authoritative completeness checks. Update this document, route tests, and ownership metadata in the same change.\n\n## Portal and Operations Routes\n\n### Tournament operations board\n\n- `GET /sports/tournament/<tournament_id>/operations`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationTournamentOperationsPortal.portal_tournament_operations_page`\n  - Downstream boundary: tournament operations access resolver\n  - Purpose: authenticated operations-board shell\n\n- `POST /sports/tournament/<tournament_id>/operations/data`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationTournamentOperationsPortal.portal_tournament_operations_data`\n  - Downstream boundary: `federation.tournament._operations_get_payload`\n  - Purpose: scoped JSON-RPC board payload\n  - Safeguards: authenticated user, CSRF, model-owned access scope\n\n- `POST /sports/tournament/<tournament_id>/operations/matches/<match_id>/action`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationTournamentOperationsPortal.portal_tournament_operations_action`\n  - Downstream boundary: tournament operations action service\n  - Purpose: result and operational match actions\n  - Safeguards: authenticated user, CSRF, tournament and match scope checks\n\n### Club self-service\n\n- `POST /my/teams/new`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationClubPortal.portal_my_teams_create`\n  - Downstream boundary: `federation.team._portal_create_team`\n\n- `POST /my/players/new`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationClubPortal.portal_my_players_create`\n  - Downstream boundary: `federation.player._portal_create_player`\n\n- `POST /my/season-registration/new`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationRegistrationPortal.portal_season_registration_submit`\n  - Downstream boundary: `federation.season.registration._portal_submit_registration_request`\n\n- `POST /my/referee-assignments/<assignment_id>/respond`\n  - Owner: `sports_federation_portal`\n  - Controller: `FederationOfficiatingPortal.portal_my_referee_assignment_respond`\n  - Downstream boundary: assignment portal access and confirmation/decline methods\n\nAll portal writes must resolve the request-user scope before calling a model-owned method. Raw controller `sudo()` writes are not an accepted boundary.\n\n## Public Routes\n\nCanonical public competition pages and match-day publication views are owned by `sports_federation_public_site` under `/competitions`. Tournament-named and numeric page routes are compatibility redirects. Versioned tournament feeds and calendars remain supported integration contracts until separately versioned.\n\nCritical public contracts include:\n\n- `GET /api/v1/tournaments/<slug>/feed`\n- `GET /tournaments/<slug>/schedule.ics`\n- `POST /tournaments/<slug>/register`\n\nPublic reads must enforce applicable publication flags.  {"content": "# State And Ownership Matrix\n\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nLast reviewed: 2026-05-20\nReview cadence: Every release\n\nThis document records the canonical lifecycle states and ownership boundaries\nfor the federation records called out in the roadmap. It is intended to keep\ncontrollers, record rules, tests, and workflow docs aligned with the code.\n\n## Canonical records\n\n| Record | Module | Primary owner | Create path | Canonical states | Ownership boundary | Notes |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `federation.tournament` | `sports_federation_tournament` + `sports_federation_portal` | Federation competition operations | Backend UI, scheduling services, website publication helpers | `draft`, `open`, `in_progress`, `closed`, `cancelled` | Federation-owned season / competition scope | Portal tournament workspaces only expose active tournaments (`open`, `in_progress`) and only for teams inside the user's current whole-club or explicit team scope. |\n| `federation.match` | `sports_federation_tournament` | Federation competition operations | Backend UI, scheduling services, wizards | `draft`, `scheduled`, `in_progress`, `done`, `cancelled` | Federation-owned tournament/stage/group | Official standings inclusion is further gated by `include_in_official_standings` when `sports_federation_result_control` is installed. |\n| `federation.schedule.slot` | `sports_federation_calendar` | Calendar operations prepare capacity; scheduling assigns fixtures; approval publishes the resulting schedule | Match-day calendar and scheduling views | `available`, `break`, `blocked` | Federation-owned match day / playing-area scope | A slot belongs to one match day. Fixture assignment and overlap validation are enforced server-side. |\n| `federation.standing` | `sports_federation_standings` | Federation competition operations | Backend UI, recompute/freeze actions | `draft`, `computed`, `frozen` | Federation-owned tournament/stage/group | Only computed or frozen standings are publication candidates. |\n| `federation.tournament.participant` | `sports_federation_tournament` | Federation competition operations | Backend UI or confirmed tournament registration | `registered`, `confirmed`, `withdrawn` | Team's club within a federation tournament | Portal users do not write participants directly; confirmed registrations may create them. |\n| `federation.competition.entry` | `sports_federation_portal` | Shared: club representative submits, federation staff reviews | Portal form or backend UI | `draft`, `submitted`, `confirmed`, `rejected`, `cancelled` | Team must belong to one of the submitting user's represented clubs | This is the review buffer before participant creation. |\n| `federation.season.registration` | `sports_federation_base` + `sports_federation_portal` | Shared: club representative submits, federation staff confirms | Portal form or backend UI | `draft`, `submitted`, `confirmed`, `cancelled` | Team must belong to one of the submitting user's represented clubs | Portal extension adds `submitted`, `user_id`, and `rejection_reason`. Rejecting a registration returns it to `draft`; there is no persistent `rejected` enum. |\n| `federation.finance.event` | `sports_federation_finance_bridge` | Federation finance operations and approved workflow hooks | Backend UI or workflow automation | `draft`, `confirmed`, `settled`, `cancelled` | Source record remains authoritative; finance staff own settlement/cancellation | Season-registration confirmation and match finance hooks create events automatically. |\n\n## Archive behavior\n\nThe core master-data records now expose explicit archive and restore actions in\nthe backend UI so operational records do not disappear through ad hoc writes to\n`active`.\n\n| Record | Archive guardrail |\n| --- | --- |\n| `federation.club` | Clubs can only be archived after their active teams are archived. |\n| `federation.team` | Teams can only be archived when all linked season registrations are cancelled. |\n| `federation.season` | Open seasons must be closed or cancelled before they can be archived. |\n| `federation.tournament` | Open or in-progress tournaments must be closed or cancelled before they can be archived. |\n\n## Transition ownership\n\n| Record | Transition owners | Guardrails |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `federation.tournament` | Federation staff for lifecycle transitions; portal layer for read-only workspace access | Portal workspace helpers must revalidate both active-tournament scope and the caller's visible team or club scope before any elevated detail read. |\n| `federation.match` | Federation staff, scheduling services, result-control roles | Public routes are read-only. Match completion may trigger bracket advancement, and result-control transitions may trigger non-frozen standings recomputation. |\n| `federation.schedule.slot` | Calendar owns slot capacity and lifecycle; scheduling assigns fixtures; schedule approval publishes the resulting schedule | Slot availability and capacity are prepared by `sports_federation_calendar`; assignment validation is enforced by `sports_federation_scheduling`; approved schedules are immutable and published by `sports_federation_schedule_approval`. |\n| `federation.standing` | Federation staff | Frozen standings block recomputation unless forced. |\n| `federation.tournament.participant` | Federation staff, confirmed registration flow | Tournament/team eligibility is validated on create and update. |\n| `federation.competition.entry` | Portal club representatives for submission/cancel, federation staff for confirm/reject | Controllers validate club ownership and the model enforces ownership as defense in depth. |\n| `federation.season.registration` | Portal club representatives for submission/cancel, federation staff for confirm | Controllers validate club ownership and the model must enforce ownership as defense in depth. |\n| `federation.finance.event` | Federation finance staff for confirm/settle/cancel; approved workflow hooks for draft creation | Auto-created events must stay idempotent per source record and fee type. |\n\n## Enum reconciliation\n\nUse the model enums below in code, docs, and tests:\n\n- Tournament lifecycle ends in `closed`, not `completed`.\n- Match lifecycle ends in `done`, not `completed`.\n- Competition planning is handed over through registration, format freeze, calendar capacity, scheduling, approval publication, and match-day execution.\n- Schedule slots use the calendar lifecycle `available`, `break`, and `blocked`; schedule approval protects published snapshots from routine edits.\n- Tournament registration review uses `submitted` and `rejected`.\n- Season registration review uses `submitted`; there is no `rejected` state in the current model.\n- Finance event lifecycle uses `settled`; `invoiced` and `paid` are future integration concepts, not current model states.\n- Seasons only open from `draft`, close from `open`, and return to `draft` from `cancelled`.\n- Tournaments only open from `draft`, start from `open`, close from `in_progress`, and return to `draft` from `cancelled`.\n\n## Review checklist\n\n- New controller writes must validate represented-club ownership before any `sudo().create()` or state change.\n- Portal tournament workspace reads and match-day record rules must revalidate active-tournament scope plus the user's current `portal_club_scope_ids` and `portal_team_scope_ids` before any elevated read or cross-team search.\n- Portal roster detail, roster-line edit, and roster-line license paths must resolve ids through the shared portal privilege boundary instead of raw elevated browse calls.\n- Public website routes must enforce `website_published` and the relevant visibility toggle before reading data with `sudo()`, and slug or numeric compatibility routes must resolve records through those publication-scoped domains up front.\n- State transitions referenced in docs and workflows must match the real selection values in the models.\n", "content_length": 7935, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "STATE_AND_OWNERSHIP_MATRIX.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "c4dba91069618fea21c73ed5c8c6ac1bdc093b8cdc3f2c9634a6cadcbebd6428", "size_bytes": 7935}
 {"content": "# Sports Federation — Technical Note\n\nLast updated: 2026-05-29\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nLast reviewed: 2026-05-25\nReview cadence: Every release\n\n## Canonical terminology and workflow authority (2026-05-27)\n\n- Use actor-facing business terms in workflows, menu labels, and helper copy.\n    Keep raw model names in technical references only.\n- Keep one authoritative source per state family. Secondary workflows should\n    link back instead of restating the full lifecycle.\n\n### Canonical terms\n\n- Competition template: reusable `federation.competition` definition shared\n    across seasons.\n- Season competition: season-scoped `federation.competition.edition` record.\n    Use this instead of `competition shell` or `edition` in operator-facing copy.\n- Division: operational `federation.tournament` record planned, staffed, and\n    published inside one season competition.\n- Tournament page: visitor-facing name for a published division on the public\n    website. Public copy may say `tournament` where federation operations copy\n    says `division`.\n- Round: logical pairing or bracket round, not the dated match day.\n- Gameday: dated `federation.tournament.round` used for venues, slots, and\n    one-day preparation.\n- Planning Workspace: primary season-competition and division scheduling\n    surface.\n- Gameday Planner: day-level slot assignment and readiness surface.\n- Registration: enrolment request. Qualify it as season registration or\n    tournament registration when needed.\n- Participant: confirmed team entry inside a division.\n- Publication: public visibility controlled through `website_published` and the\n    relevant visibility toggles. Not every public surface needs a dedicated\n    `published` workflow state.\n\n### Authoritative state references\n\n- Result officiality: `draft -> submitted -> verified -> approved`, with\n    `contested` and `corrected` removing the result from official standings until\n    it is resubmitted and approved again. Source:\n    `sports_federation_result_control/models/match_result_control.py` and\n    `WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md`.\n- Standings lifecycle: `draft -> computed -> frozen`. Public visibility is\n    separate through `website_published`; there is no dedicated published\n    standings state. Source: `sports_federation_standings/models/standing.py` and\n    `sports_federation_standings/README.md`.\n- Player license lifecycle: `draft -> active -> expired` or `cancelled`, with\n    `action_draft()` available for controlled correction. Use `cancelled` in\n    operator copy; older `revoked` wording is compatibility-only. Source:\n    `sports_federation_people/models/federation_player_license.py` and\n    `WORKFLOW_PLAYER_LICENSE.md`.\n- Public competition routes: `/competitions/...` is the canonical public route\n    family. `/competitions...` and numeric routes are compatibility paths only\n    and should not be used in normal operator guidance. Source:\n    `sports_federation_public_site/controllers/public_competitions.py` and\n    `WORKFLOW_PUBLIC_PUBLICATION.md`.\n\n## Navigation baseline (2026-05-25)\n\n- Federation backend navigation is now grouped by operator journey instead of a\n    flat addon list: `Setup`, `Planning`, `Match Day`, `Publication`, and\n    `Administration`.\n- `Planning Workspace` is the primary backend scheduling entry point. Legacy\n    round-robin and knockout generators remain available on tournament records,\n    but the UI now labels them as advanced wizard paths.\n- Club representatives now land on `Club Operations Workspace` as the primary\n    portal start for registrations, rosters, match-day submissions, and result\n    follow-up. Direct queues still exist, but the UI frames them as secondary or\n    advanced paths.\n- Match-day portal labels now reinforce one phase at a time: `Preparation Queue`\n    for pre-kick-off work, `Live Operations Board` for in-progress play, and\n    `Result Follow-Up Queue` for post-match review. Match sheets and raw match\n    records remain direct-access specialist surfaces rather than peer home\n    destinations.\n\n## Guided setup and cross-module handoffs (2026-05-25)\n\n- `sports_federation_base` and `sports_federation_tournament` now use inline\n    banner guidance and richer action help on season, competition,\n    competition-edition, and tournament forms so empty states describe the next\n    valid setup action instead of leaving operators on unexplained lists.\n- `sports_federation_rosters` adds direct operator handoffs from confirmed\n    season registrations into scoped team rosters and from tournament\n    participants into the roster used for readiness checks. The participant form\n    now exposes the roster deadline, current readiness feedback, and the\n    preferred readiness roster in the same place where confirmation can block.\n- `sports_federation_result_control` adds a tournament handoff on match forms so\n    verified, approved, contested, and corrected results can direct staff back\n    to the owning tournament when the next task is standings review or public\n    follow-up rather than another score edit.\n- `sports_federation_standings` and `sports_federation_public_site` now make\n    publication prerequisites visible from the forms themselves: standings pages\n    explain whether approved results are still missing or whether publication is\n    the next step, while tournament and standings Website tabs differentiate\n    between unpublished tournaments, unpublished standings, and hidden\n    standings, with direct links back to the relevant standings action.\n\nCanonical readiness and handoff chain:\n\n1. Season registration confirmation\n2. Tournament participant confirmation\n3. Roster and match-day readiness\n4. Result approval\n5. Standings review/recompute\n6. Tournament and standings publication\n\nBlocking vs warning-only standard:\n\n- Blocking: current step cannot proceed until resolved.\n- Warning-only: current step can proceed, but follow-up is required; manager\n    override reason is mandatory where configured (for example publication\n    publication overrides in `sports_federation_schedule_approval`).\n\nException and publication recovery standard:\n\n- Governance Override is the canonical exception engine.\n- After disputed/corrected outcomes, use one recovery loop: resolve exception,\n    restore result/standings officiality, then republish affected public surfaces.\n\n## Canonical planning and match-day flows (2026-05-25)\n\n- `sports_federation_registration` finalizes participant sets for a competition.\n- `sports_federation_format` freezes the versioned logical structure and stage\n    graph.\n- `sports_federation_calendar` prepares physical match days, capacity, and slots.\n- `sports_federation_scheduling` validates and proposes deterministic fixture\n    assignments.\n- `sports_federation_schedule_approval` reviews schedules and publishes an\n    immutable snapshot; `sports_federation_matchday` executes only that snapshot.\n- `sports_federation_portal` now names three distinct club-operator phases:\n    match-day preparation in the workspace or preparation queue, live play in\n    the live operations board, and result follow-up in the results queue. Those\n    portal surfaces now explain when operators should move from one phase to the\n    next instead of presenting the routes as equal-weight peers.\n\n## competition planning flow (2026-05-25)\n\nThe competition workflow is implemented by small, role-owned addons rather\nthan a monolithic workspace service. Each handover is explicit and the server\nremains authoritative for validation and publication.\n\n### Phase completion snapshot\n\n- Registration finalizes a participant set before format work begins.\n- Format creates a versioned structure and stage graph; it does not create\n    physical slots.\n- Calendar creates physical match days and slots from capacity and timeline\n    rules.\n- Scheduling accepts the frozen structure and calendar capacity, then writes\n    validated fixture-to-slot assignments through its server-side commands.\n- Approval is a separate review boundary and stores the immutable publication\n    snapshot. Match-day commands reject unpublished or superseded schedules.\n- Portal and officiating modules extend their own surfaces without becoming the\n    authoritative owner of competition transitions.\n\n### Retired competition-core view cleanup (2026-08-27)\n\nCompetition Core version `19.0.1.3.0` removes the database UI records left\nbehind by the retired competition engine before current division views are\nvalidated. This is required because Odoo does not automatically delete records\nwhose XML IDs disappear from a module's data files. The migration is\nidempotent and preserves the current role-assignment and competition-history\nactions.\n\n## Portal/public intuitiveness baseline (2026-05-26)\n\n- `sports_federation_portal/models/portal_status_labels.py` now centralizes\n    human-facing labels for tournament, registration, participant, match,\n    result-state, sheet, officiating assignment, duty, player, and license\n    states.\n- Portal templates now render state labels via model helper methods instead of\n    direct selection dictionaries or raw `state`/`result_state` values.\n- `sports_federation_public_site/models/public_status_labels.py` now includes\n    public-facing license state labels so club/player public pages avoid raw\n    internal status rendering.\n- Regression guards in portal/public template accessibility tests now block\n    reintroduction of raw internal state render fragments.\n- Intuitiveness governance follows the documentation and terminology review\n    expectations in `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\n\nValidation policy\n\n- Slot occupancy conflicts and team overlap conflicts are blocking.\n- A slot occupancy conflict remains blocking for unscheduled matches, but two\n    already scheduled matches on the same planner root can swap slots when both\n    post-swap validations pass.\n- Short-rest checks use `minimum_rest_minutes` on the division and surface as\n    warnings, not blocking errors.\n- Warning-only assignment overrides and republishing a live schedule require a\n    manager reason that is stored on the planner operation or schedule\n    revision.\n- Validation payloads now include grouped conflict sections, resolution hints,\n    and focus metadata so the UI can highlight the affected slots and matches.\n- Published gamedays protect routine schedule edits for planners.\n- Regression coverage now verifies single and double round-robin generation,\n    knockout generation, locked entry requirements, manager-only competition\n    creation, create-competition input validation, slot generation, blocking\n    overlap rules, short-rest warnings, assignment consistency, publish guards,\n    workspace access control, shared-gameday assignment and planner payload\n    rules, revisioned publication, collaboration heartbeat summaries, grouped\n    validation payloads, and a browser smoke path that creates a competition\n    through the Owl client action.\n\n## Import safety hardening (2026-04-10)\n\nYear 1 Priority 1 item 7 hardened the onboarding and rollover import path in\n`sports_federation_import_tools`.\n\n- All import wizards now share `federation.import.wizard.mixin`, which provides\n    UTF-8 BOM handling, comma or semicolon delimiter support, mapping guidance,\n    shared dry-run statistics, and categorized row-level failure reporting.\n- A new `federation.import.seasons.wizard` covers season creation for annual\n    rollover work, including required-code enforcement and `YYYY-MM-DD` date\n    validation.\n- Club, team, player, and tournament-participant imports now prefer code-based\n    matching when codes are available, which aligns the implementation with the\n    documented onboarding workflow.\n- The player import path no longer attempts to create `federation.player`\n    records with a nonexistent writable `name` field. Imports now populate\n    `first_name` and `last_name`, while still accepting legacy full-name CSVs\n    when they contain both parts.\n- Duplicate rows are intentionally reported and skipped rather than overwritten,\n    so administrators can rehearse and rerun seasonal imports safely.\n\n## Operational reporting expansion (2026-04-10)\n\nYear 1 Priority 1 item 6 expanded reporting from raw CSV extraction into\noperator-facing application reports.\n\n- `sports_federation_reporting` now includes `federation.report.operational`\n    for tournament readiness KPIs across participation, standings coverage,\n    match completion, finance follow-up, and participant-club compliance.\n- Operational KPI rows now include an operator-readable readiness note so the\n    list view and scheduled CSV exports expose the current blocker directly,\n    instead of forcing staff to infer it from raw counts alone.\n- A new `federation.report.standing.reconciliation` view exposes mismatches\n    between confirmed participants and standings coverage, with operator-readable\n    reconciliation notes.\n- A new `federation.report.finance.reconciliation` view turns finance events\n    into a follow-up queue keyed by counterparty, references, age, and\n    completion status instead of only grouped fee totals.\n- `federation.report.schedule` plus a daily cron now generate recurring weekly\n    or monthly CSV snapshots from inside Odoo, so administrators can run the\n    reporting cadence without direct database access.\n- The compliance summary report now exposes `non_compliant_count` so rejected\n    or explicitly failed checks are visible alongside missing, pending, and\n    expired totals.\n\n## Scheduled reporting maintainability extraction (2026-04-21)\n\nThe next highest-value reporting cleanup was to separate schedule orchestration\nfrom per-report row assembly.\n\n- `sports_federation_reporting/services/report_schedule_builders.py` now owns\n    the scheduled-report registry: label metadata, report-row builder\n    functions, list-action XML IDs, season scoping, and any action defaults.\n- `federation.report.schedule` now keeps the stable responsibilities only:\n    cadence windows, CSV serialization, generated-file retention, and typed\n    failure capture for cron or manual runs.\n- Adding a new scheduled report type should now be a single-registry change\n    plus focused tests, instead of editing separate builder and action maps in\n    the model.\n\n## Notification activation (2026-04-10)\n\nYear 1 Priority 1 item 5 converted the notification module from mostly logged\nplaceholders into active workflow delivery.\n\n- `sports_federation_notifications` now sends real emails for season\n    registration decisions, tournament publication, participant confirmation,\n    approved and contested results, frozen standings, finance confirmations,\n    and referee assignments.\n- Result submission now creates validator activities instead of a placeholder\n    log entry, and the scheduled notification scan now creates federation\n    manager activities for overdue referee confirmations and officiating\n    shortages.\n- Shared notification helpers now accept multi-recipient email payloads,\n    deduplicate them, and keep failures non-blocking by writing `failed`\n    notification-log rows instead of rolling back the business action.\n- Suspension issuance remains the only modeled scenario still left as a stub.\n\n## Match-day roster traceability (2026-04-10)\n\nYear 1 Priority 1 item 2 closed the operational gap between season rosters,\nmatch sheets, portal visibility, and disputed results.\n\n- `sports_federation_rosters` now logs roster, lineup, and substitution events\n    in `federation.participation.audit`.\n- Season rosters now expose match-day locking state. Once a live match sheet\n    references a roster line, that referenced line cannot be structurally changed\n    or removed.\n- Approved match sheets now freeze lineup edits while still allowing\n    substitution timing (`entered_minute`, `left_minute`) until final lock.\n- `sports_federation_result_control` now logs every result transition in\n    `federation.match.result.audit`, including dispute and correction reasons.\n- `sports_federation_portal` now exposes read-only roster and match-sheet pages\n    so club representatives can review eligibility blocks, substitutions,\n    participation audit history, and result disputes from the portal.\n\n## Eligibility workflow hardening (2026-04-10)\n\nYear 1 Priority 1 item 1 connected the existing eligibility models to live\nworkflow boundaries instead of leaving them as isolated checks.\n\n- `sports_federation_rules` now evaluates license rules with season, club,\n    selected-license, and reference-date context. Registration rules validate the\n    actual `federation.season.registration` team/season model rather than\n    non-existent player-level registration fields.\n- `sports_federation_rosters` now computes `ready_for_activation` and\n    `readiness_feedback` on rosters. Activation blocks until the active lines meet\n    the rule-set size limits and all players pass eligibility checks.\n- `sports_federation_rosters` now computes `ready_for_submission` and\n    `readiness_feedback` on match sheets. Submission blocks when players are not\n    tied to valid active roster lines, do not satisfy license or registration\n    rules, or the submitted squad size is outside the allowed bounds.\n- `sports_federation_rosters` extends `federation.tournament.participant` so\n    participant confirmation warns before the roster deadline, then requires an\n    active ready roster once that deadline is reached for the tournament\n    season, with competition-specific rosters preferred over generic season\n    rosters.\n- Regression coverage was added for season-aware license checks, team-season\n    registration checks, roster activation gating, match-sheet submission gating,\n    and participant confirmation readiness.\n\n## New competition models and behaviours (2026-04-07)\n\nSeveral new models and scheduling behaviours were added to support more realistic tournaments and automated stage progression:\n\n### Structure stage graph compatibility\n\nThe `sports_federation_format` addon owns the versioned structure graph used by Format Studio. Its stage-to-stage edges are represented by `federation.structure.stage.progression`, with `structure_id`, source-stage, and target-stage relations. This model must not reuse the competition engine's `federation.stage.progression` name: the engine model is tournament-oriented and has a different inverse relation contract (`tournament_id`). The distinction allows both addons to be installed in the same registry without field-resolution collisions.\n\nStage-graph ACLs are loaded through the addon's conventional `security/ir.model.access.csv` file. Format Studio's inherited view selects the stage tab structurally by its `stage_ids` field rather than by the translated page label.\n\n- `federation.tournament.round` (in `sports_federation_tournament`) — materialises a logical round inside a stage. Rounds own shared schedule metadata such as sequence and calendar date, and matches attach to them for per-round planning and reporting.\n\n- Round venue extensions (in `sports_federation_venues`) — add `venue_id` to `federation.tournament.round` so venue ownership sits on the same round object that already owns the schedule date and sequence.\n\n- Match bracket/linking fields — `federation.match` now includes: `round_id`, `round_number`, `bracket_position`, `bracket_type`, `source_match_1_id`, `source_match_2_id`, `source_type_1/2` and helper `next_match_ids`. These turn matches into first-class bracket nodes so full knockout trees can be created and wired together programmatically.\n\n- Full knockout bracket generation — the knockout service now constructs a full bracket tree (all rounds) rather than only first-round matches. Placeholder matches for later rounds are created and linked to their source matches (by `source_match_*`); when a match is completed the system will auto-populate winners/losers into the next round using the `action_done()` / `_advance_bracket_teams()` logic.\n\n- Round-robin enhancements — the round-robin service now returns explicit per-round pairings, supports `rounds_count` (repeat full cycles), always materialises `federation.tournament.round` records, and can derive round dates from `schedule_by_round`. Existing stage rounds are reused by sequence order, and when a `venue` string matches a `federation.venue` record the scheduler applies it to the round and linked matches. The service also attempts to alternate `male` / `female` fixtures within a round to provide rest.\n\nImplementation notes\n\n- Round constraints: the `sports_federation_venues` module extends `federation.match` so teams in the same `category` cannot play the same opponent more than once inside the same round. This prevents repeated pairings inside a shared round block.\n\n- Alternation policy: when scheduling by round the scheduler partitions same-gender fixtures and interleaves male/female matches where possible. Mixed-gender fixtures are appended after the alternation. This is a best-effort policy (it preserves determinism but will not re-seed teams).\n\n- Auto-advance: `federation.standing.action_freeze()` triggers any `federation.stage.progression` rules with `auto_advance=True` for the stage/group being frozen — this provides an automated pipeline from computed standings into new stage participants (and is safe-guarded by the progression rule's `state`).\n\n- Venue handling: the `venue` free-text field has been removed from `federation.match`. Use `venue_id` (Many2one to `federation.venue`) instead. The round-robin and knockout wizards accept a venue name string and resolve it to a `federation.venue` record to populate the generated rounds and linked matches.\n\nTesting and coverage\n\nAdd unit and integration tests covering the following:\n\n- `federation.stage.progression`: ensure `action_execute()` selects the correct teams across single-group and cross-group rules and that `seeding_method` options behave as documented.\n- `federation.tournament.template`: applying a template creates the expected stages, groups and progression rules.\n- Round-robin scheduling: `rounds_count`, `schedule_by_round`, automatic round materialisation, alternation of male/female fixtures, and repeat cycles.\n- Knockout bracket generation: bracket size determination, bye handling, and automatic wiring of placeholder matches and advancement on `action_done()`.\n- Round constraints: enforce no duplicate same-category pairings inside the same round.\n\n## Architecture and conventions\n\nThis technical note documents architecture, coding conventions, workflows, and extension points for the Sports Federation Odoo 19 custom addons collection located in the `odoo/` folder. It is intended for developers, integrators, and release engineers working on federation features: tournaments, matches, rosters, refereeing, results pipelines, and public website publication.\n\n### Shared workflow state helpers (2026-04-18)\n\nWhere one addon reuses the same workflow vocabulary across multiple models,\nkeep the selection tuples and state-role predicates in a small plain Python\nhelper module instead of repeating raw string tuples in each model.\n\n- `sports_federation_import_tools/workflow_states.py` now owns the inbound\n    delivery and import-governance lifecycle constants used by the delivery\n    model, governance job model, wizard mixin, migration backfill, and tests.\n- `sports_federation_governance/workflow_states.py` now owns the override\n    request and decision lifecycle constants used by the request, decision,\n    outcome, and test modules.\n- When new logic only needs to know the semantic role of a state, prefer a\n    helper predicate such as `is_import_job_approved(...)` over direct string\n    comparisons so future state changes stay localized.\n\n## Table of contents\n\n- Executive summary\n- High-level architecture and module responsibilities\n- Data model conventions and patterns\n- Workflows and state machines (tournament lifecycle, match-day, result pipeline)\n- Competition engine — algorithms, wizards, and deterministic behaviours\n- Controllers, portal, and public site patterns\n- Security model and record rules\n- Performance, scaling and operational concerns\n- Testing, CI and quality gates\n- Upgrade, migrations and deployment notes\n- Developer workflow: how to add models, wizards, views, and tests\n- Examples and reference snippets\n\n## Executive summary\n\nThe project is a modular suite of Odoo 19 addons implementing a sports federation management system. Modules are intentionally small and focused: `sports_federation_base` owns master data (clubs, teams, seasons), `sports_federation_tournament` implements tournament structure and match records, and the competition addons own registration, format, calendar, scheduling, approval, and match-day operations. Domain features (`people`, `rosters`, `officiating`, `result_control`, `standings`, `public_site`, `notifications`, `reporting`) extend core behaviour without mixing responsibilities.\n\nDesign goals\n\n- Keep modules decoupled and installable independently where practical.\n- Encode business logic in computed models and service/wizard classes rather than ad-hoc scripts.\n- Enforce separation of duties via groups and record rules (submit/verify/approve in the result pipeline).\n- Make scheduling deterministic and testable (repeatable outputs from the same inputs).\n\n## High-level architecture and module responsibilities\n\n- `sports_federation_base` — Master data: `federation.club`, `federation.team`, `federation.season`, `federation.season.registration`. Ownership of base security groups and global menus. Holds common `ir.sequence` definitions.\n- `sports_federation_tournament` — Tournament structure: `federation.competition`, `federation.tournament`, `federation.tournament.stage`, `federation.group`, `federation.tournament.participant`, and `federation.match`. Implements match lifecycle and basic match behaviour.\n- `sports_federation_format` and `sports_federation_scheduling` — Structure generation, deterministic fixture assignment, validation, and fairness services. Keep algorithmic code stateless and place transient entrypoints in the owning module's `wizards/` package.\n- `sports_federation_people` — Player and license models used for eligibility checks and rosters.\n- `sports_federation_rosters` — Season rosters and match sheet management.\n- `sports_federation_officiating` — Referee registry and assignment workflows.\n- `sports_federation_finance_bridge` — Lightweight finance events with source-linked,\n  idempotent hooks for registrations, result approval, sanctions, officiating,\n  and venue settlements.\n- `sports_federation_notifications` — Central email/activity dispatcher and\n    audit log for registration, publication, result, officiating, standings, and\n    finance workflow events.\n- `sports_federation_result_control` — Result submission, verification, approval, contest and correction flows.\n- `sports_federation_standings` — Standings computation, tie-break logic, and publishing controls.\n- `sports_federation_public_site` / `sports_federation_portal` — Website and portal layers for public pages and club self-service.\n\nInter-module rules\n\n- Use `depends` in `__manifest__.py` for compile-time coupling and prefer runtime loose coupling in service code.\n- Avoid importing models directly across modules in shared utils; reference them through the ORM (`env['federation.model']`).\n\nSecurity-group compatibility\n\n- Odoo 19 classifies application groups through `res.groups.privilege` records. Addons must assign a group with `privilege_id`; `category_id` belongs on the privilege and is no longer a valid field on `res.groups`.\n- Federation privileges should reference an existing module category such as `base.module_category_services` and remain in the addon security XML beside the groups they classify. This preserves the access-rights UI grouping without relying on the removed pre-Odoo-19 group field.\n\n## Data model conventions and patterns\n\n- Names: all domain models use the `federation.` prefix.\n- Fields: prefer explicit names (`home_score`, `away_score`, `match_date`, `venue_id`). Use `_id` for Many2one fields.\n- Sequences: centralize `ir.sequence` definitions under the modules that issue numbers (licenses, registrations, match references).\n- States: encode lifecycle using `state` selection fields. Implement transition methods (e.g., `action_confirm()`) that include invariant checks and post-transition side-effects (message_post, cron triggers).\n- Constraints and indexes: use `_sql_constraints` for DB-level uniqueness and `index=True` on frequently filtered fields.\n\nORM best practices\n\n- Use `@api.depends` for computed fields; mark heavy computed fields with `store=True` when the value is frequently filtered or sorted.\n- Use `read_group` for group-by aggregations instead of computing aggregates in Python loops.\n- When creating many records (e.g., fixtures for a large tournament), batch creations in transactions and avoid per-record flushes where possible.\n\n## Workflows and state machines\n\nThe canonical workflows live in `odoo/_workflows` (authoritative). Implementation notes below reflect the codebase expectations.\n\nTournament lifecycle\n\n- States: `draft → open → in_progress → closed | cancelled`.\n- Preconditions for `open`: participants registered/confirmed, ruleset assigned, optional venues configured.\n- Schedule creation typically moves the tournament to `in_progress` (explicit action required).\n- Archive and restore are explicit backend actions. Open or in-progress tournaments must be closed or cancelled before archiving, and only active draft tournaments linked to a season may be opened.\n\nMatch lifecycle and match-day operations\n\n- States: `draft → scheduled → in_progress → done | cancelled`.\n- Pre-match checks: both teams have confirmed match-sheets, required referee roles filled and confirmed, no suspensions on selected players, venue confirmed.\n- Officiating readiness is computed directly on the match from assignment state, rule-set referee counts, overdue confirmations, and referee certification validity so operators can filter or inspect staffing gaps without opening each assignment.\n- During match: record events (substitutions, cards) which feed the discipline and finance modules.\n\nResult pipeline\n\n- States: `not_submitted → submitted → verified → approved` (exceptions: `contested`, `corrected`).\n- Permissions: separate groups for submit/verify/approve to enforce audit and separation of duties.\n- Approved results are the only ones included in official standings computations.\n- The same user must not submit, verify, and approve the same result. Self-verification is blocked and approvers cannot approve their own submissions or the result they verified.\n- Approving, contesting, correcting, or resetting a result triggers automatic recomputation of linked non-frozen standings. Approved scores become immutable until the result is moved out of the approved state.\n\n## Competition engine — algorithms and wizards\n\nPrinciples\n\n- Determinism: given the same input (participant list, seeding, start datetime) the schedule generation must produce the same fixtures. Sort input participants by an unambiguous key (id or explicit seeding) before generation.\n- Testability: isolate algorithms in service classes and unit-test them with small deterministic datasets.\n\nRound-robin (circle method)\n\n- Supports odd/even participant counts (auto bye round for odd counts) and single/double round-robin.\n- Complexity: O(n^2) matches for n participants (n*(n-1)/2 for single round-robin).\n\nExample pseudocode (circle method)\n\n```python\ndef generate_round_robin(participants, double=False):\n    participants = sorted(participants, key=lambda p: p.seeding or p.id)\n    if len(participants) % 2 == 1:\n        participants.append(None)  # bye\n    n = len(participants)\n    rounds = []\n    for r in range(n - 1):\n        pairs = []\n        for i in range(n // 2):\n            a = participants[i]\n            b = participants[n - 1 - i]\n            if a is not None and b is not None:\n                pairs.append((a, b))\n        rounds.append(pairs)\n        participants = [participants[0]] + participants[-1:] + participants[1:-1]\n    if double:\n        return rounds + [reverse_pairs(r) for r in rounds]\n    return rounds\n```\n\nKnockout bracket generation\n\n- Seeding strategies: `natural` (order of participants) and `power_of_two` (map to nearest bracket size and set byes).\n- Ensure bracket seeding is deterministic and documented in the UI.\n\nWizards\n\n- Wizards (transient models under `wizards/`) do validations, produce a `summary` preview, and require explicit confirmation to persist matches. They must not perform destructive replacements unless the user explicitly enables `overwrite`.\n- Competition-generation wizards validate tournament state, the effective rule set, minimum participant counts, and stage/group ownership before creating matches. Overwrite mode is warning-first and should remain opt-in.\n\n## Controllers, portal, and public site patterns\n\nPortal patterns\n\n- Use a dedicated `federation.club.representative` model to map `res.users` → `federation.club` for portal ownership and record rules.\n- Controllers must perform ownership validation (`_get_clubs_for_user()`) before writes. Record rules are enforcement, controllers are defense-in-depth.\n- ORM-level ownership constraints should mirror controller checks for portal-created registrations so bypassing a controller does not widen access.\n- The tournament operations board (`/sports/tournament/<id>/operations`) extends these patterns with an Owl frontend. Portal access requires provable tournament-scoped activity (registration, participant, or visible match). Writes delegate to existing result-control action methods. The Owl app uses standalone `mount()` from `@odoo/owl`, not the backend webclient `mountComponent`, since it runs inside the website/portal frontend context. It mounts immediately so the board can render its own loading and error states, applies a 30-second timeout to the first payload fetch, and relies on a server-side payload that includes ranked action-queue rows, court summaries, next-step hints, and schedule labels so the browser stays presentation-focused.\n\nPublic site\n\n- Public controllers use `auth='public'` and `sudo()` for reads.
