Only expose non-sensitive fields (no emails/phones/notes) to public templates.\n- Enforce `website_published` and the relevant visibility toggle (`show_public_results`, `show_public_standings`) before serving direct public routes.\n- When a controller must deny a hidden portal or public route with 404, raise the HTTP exception (`raise request.not_found()`) instead of returning it, so fail-closed paths stay quiet in logs and match Odoo's expected flow.\n- Render public rich text through sanitized website field rendering rather than raw `t-raw` output.\n- Keep `public_slug` unique even while the public routes remain model-bound, so publication metadata and future external references cannot collide.\n\nCSRF and forms\n\n- All POST forms must include `csrf_token` and controllers must validate it.\n\n## Security model and record rules\n\nKey principles\n\n- Least privilege: create narrowly-scoped groups and map CRUD rights in `security/ir.model.access.csv`.\n- Record rules: express row-level ownership using domains (e.g., `('club_id','in', user.representative_ids.mapped('club_id').ids)`).\n- Avoid uncontrolled `sudo()` on write operations.\n\nGroups and separation of duties\n\n- Federation Staff (submit results)\n- Result Verifier (verify submitted results)\n- Result Approver (approve verified results)\n- Referee Coordinator (assign referees)\n- Portal Club Representative (portal users)\n\n## Performance, scaling and operational concerns\n\nLarge tournaments and match volumes\n\n- Match generation: create matches in batches using `create()` on list-of-dicts to minimize ORM overhead. Consider raw SQL insert for extremely large volumes but keep referential integrity and Odoo cache considerations in mind.\n- Indexes: ensure `tournament_id`, `stage_id`, `group_id`, `match_date`, and `state` fields are indexed.\n- Aggregation: compute standings incrementally or via nightly cron for large datasets. Prefer `read_group` for real-time small aggregate queries.\n\nBackground and asynchronous work\n\n- Use `ir.cron` for scheduled recomputes. For heavy parallel work consider `queue_job` or an external worker queue.\n\nMemory and worker tuning\n\n- For large batch jobs run them on a dedicated worker with increased memory limits and tuned `limit_time_cpu` and `limit_time_real` values.\n\n## Testing, CI and quality gates\n\nTest types\n\n- Unit tests: service classes and algorithms (no DB or minimal mock DB). Keep these fast and deterministic.\n- Integration tests: Odoo testcases exercising ORM-level behaviour and workflows.\n- Functional tests: simulate portal and public flows (controller-level) when needed.\n\nCI recommendations\n\n- Run module tests in CI and fail PRs on test regressions.\n- Include a lint step (flake8/black for Python where applicable) and XML/manifest validation.\n- The repository CI entrypoint is `ci/run_tests.sh`; the GitHub workflow at `.github/workflows/ci.yml` reuses that script and runs Black/Flake8 from `requirements.txt`.\n- `ci/run_tests.sh` also provides named suites (`competition_core`, `portal_public_ops`, `finance_reporting`) so maintainers can run the same focused coverage locally and in GitHub Actions.\n- The runner supports a frontend-only mode (`--frontend-module`) that performs XML/QWeb parsing, JavaScript syntax and QUnit-registration checks, asset checks, portal accessibility/mobile checks, and selected HTTP tests without scheduling or standings suites.\n- `--affected-from <ref> --include-dependents` resolves changed addons from git and expands their manifest reverse-dependency closure before testing.\n- Each run retains `test_failures.log`, `expected_diagnostics.log`, `infrastructure.log`, and `full.log`; `--keep-on-failure` additionally preserves runtime artifacts and writes exact inspection commands.\n- Public-site, portal, and reporting performance hotspots should carry explicit `assertQueryCount(...)` budgets in their regression suites; the current enforced budgets are summarized in `TESTING_GUIDE.md`.\n- Do not commit runtime credentials. Keep local CI values in `ci/.env`, commit only `ci/.env.example`, and let CI generate ephemeral values at runtime.\n- Integration environment variables: maintain `ci/integrations.env.example` with the common external-integration keys (SMTP, SendGrid/Mailgun keys, OAuth client IDs/secrets, Slack webhook, Twilio credentials, AWS S3 keys). Keep real values out of VCS and populate `ci/.env` locally or via your secret store in CI runs.\n- Contributor-facing local setup, suite selection, and script validation commands live in `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\n\nExample test command\n\n```bash\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\nbash ./ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\n```\n\n## Upgrade, migrations and deployment notes\n\nManifest and data maintenance\n\n- Keep `__manifest__.py` `version` and `data` accurate. Prefer small, focused upgrade scripts to large one-off DB migrations.\n- For breaking model changes provide a dedicated migration script (`openupgrade` style) and document steps.\n- `ci/check_migration_review.py` should pass whenever model, view, or controller ownership changes are present. Treat a passing run as evidence that migration scripts or release-note surfaces were updated in the same change set.\n- Migration-sensitive branches must record dry-run evidence and rollback notes in `RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md`.\n- `ci/check_constraint_index_contracts.py` validates curated constraint/index contracts for major workflow models. Keep `ci/contracts/constraint_index_contracts.json` aligned when those models evolve.\n- `ci/check_openapi_contracts.py` validates managed integration and public-feed OpenAPI contracts plus required golden examples (`openapi/examples/INTEGRATION_GOLDEN_EXAMPLES.md`).\n- Standings recompute now supports asynchronous, idempotent queue execution through `federation.standing.recompute.job`; use correlation IDs (`federation_correlation_id`) when invoking queue workers or replaying failed jobs.\n- Integration API, scheduled reporting crons, and notification flows propagate correlation IDs for cross-module incident tracing.\n- Keep `ROADMAP.md` and `RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md` on the same active `Release train:` value so roadmap commitments, upgrade handling, and operator checklists stay synchronized.\n\nPre/post hooks\n\n- Use `pre_init_hook` and `post_init_hook` (registered in `__manifest__`) for data transformations where required. Keep hooks idempotent and well-tested.\n\nBackups and rollback\n\n- Always perform full DB and filestore backups prior to upgrades. Test restore paths in your staging environment.\n\n## Developer workflow: add/modify checklist\n\nArchitecture decisions\n\n- Repository-level architectural choices that cut across portal trust\n    boundaries, reporting SQL views, or public route ownership live in `adr/`.\n- Update the relevant ADR in the same change set when one of those core\n    decisions materially changes.\n\nWhen introducing a new feature follow this minimal patch checklist:\n\n1. Add model code: `models/<file>.py`. Export in `models/__init__.py`.\n2. Add `security/ir.model.access.csv` and any `security/*.xml` record rules.\n3. Add views: tree/form/search under `views/*.xml` and register in `__manifest__.py` `data`.\n4. Add sequences/data seeds under `data/*.xml`.\n5. Write tests under `tests/` covering the main business rule.\n6. Update module `README.md` with short usage notes.\n7. Run tests locally; run `odoo-bin -d <db> -i <module> --test-enable`.\n8. Keep `STATE_AND_OWNERSHIP_MATRIX.md` aligned with any lifecycle or portal-ownership change.\n\nPR checklist (required)\n\n- Tests included and passing\n- `security/ir.model.access.csv` updated\n- `__manifest__.py` updated (if needed)\n- Documentation/README updated\n- Small, focused commits and descriptive PR message\n\n## Examples and reference snippets\n\nWizard skeleton (python)\n\n```python\nclass FederationRoundRobinWizard(models.TransientModel):\n    _name = 'federation.round.robin.wizard'\n    _description = 'Round Robin Schedule Wizard'\n\n    tournament_id = fields.Many2one('federation.tournament', required=True)\n    use_all_participants = fields.Boolean(default=True)\n    summary = fields.Text(compute='_compute_summary')\n\n    def action_generate(self):\n        service = RoundRobinService(self.tournament_id)\n        schedule = service.generate()\n        # create matches in batches\n        match_vals = []\n        for round in schedule:\n            for a, b in round:\n                match_vals.append({\n                    'tournament_id': self.tournament_id.id,\n                    'home_team_id': a.id,\n                    'away_team_id': b.id,\n                    'state': 'scheduled',\n                })\n        self.env['federation.match'].create(match_vals)\n\n```\n\nSQL constraints example (in model):\n\n```python\n_sql_constraints = [\n    ('unique_match_participants', 'UNIQUE(tournament_id, home_team_id, away_team_id, match_date)', 'Duplicate match')\n]\n```\n\n## Observability and logging\n\n- Use named loggers: `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` and log at appropriate levels (INFO for normal milestones, WARNING for recoverable issues, ERROR for exceptions).\n- Use `message_post` on records for an audit trail of user-driven state changes.\n\n## Security reminders\n\n- Avoid using `sudo()` to bypass authorization for write operations. If `sudo()` is necessary (controller reading public data), restrict fields returned and validate inputs carefully.\n\n---\n\nRelated docs and entry points\n\n- High-level context: [odoo/CONTEXT.md](odoo/CONTEXT.md#L1)\n- Workflows (authoritative): [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md#L1), [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md#L1), [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md#L1)\n- competition architecture: [odoo/COMPETITION_ENGINE.md](odoo/COMPETITION_ENGINE.md#L1)\n\nIf you'd like, I can now: (1) open a PR draft for this change, (2) run a repo scan of `README` and `INSTALL_LOG` files and fold additional implementation notes into this document, or (3) run the tests for a selected module to validate there are no immediate regressions.\n", "content_length": 44397, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "TECHNICAL_NOTE.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "82946be37c8d805d615ead19c8deda6d92ccbea9ca70bfbe607d9495fcd1a274", "size_bytes": 44457}
-{"content": "# Testing Guide\n\nLast updated: 2026-05-18\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\n\nThis guide explains the project's test taxonomy, how to write tests for each\ncategory, and how to keep query-budget assertions current.\n\nPerformance budgets and explain snapshots are maintained here with the test\npatterns below. A query-count increase is a regression unless the test and the\nbudget are updated together with a reason.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Test Taxonomy\n\nThe project uses three test classes depending on what is being tested:\n\n### `TransactionCase` — ORM-level unit and integration tests\n\nUsed for: model methods, computed fields, `@api.constrains` validators,\nwizards, services, and controller methods that are tested by injecting a mock\n\nEach test runs inside a savepoint that is rolled back after the test, so data\n\nfrom odoo.tests.common import TransactionCase\n\n\n    @classmethod\n    def setUpClass(cls):\n        super().setUpClass()\n        cls.club = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\n            \"name\": \"Test Club\",\n            \"code\": \"TC01\",\n        })\n\n    def test_code_must_be_unique(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(Exception):\n            self.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\"name\": \"Dup\", \"code\": \"TC01\"})\n            self.env.cr.flush()\n```\n\n**Put shared fixtures in `setUpClass`.** Fixtures created there are shared\nacross all test methods (and rolled back as a unit at the end of the class).\nFixtures created inside individual test methods are rolled back after each\nmethod.\n### `HttpCase` — end-to-end HTTP smoke tests\n\nUsed for: verifying that routes return the expected HTTP status code, CSRF\ntoken handling, portal login flows.\n\nRequires a real database and a running Odoo HTTP server. These tests must be\ntagged `@tagged(\"-at_install\", \"post_install\")` so they run after all modules\nare loaded.\n\n```python\nfrom odoo.tests.common import HttpCase, tagged\n\n@tagged(\"-at_install\", \"post_install\")\nclass TestPublicSiteHttpSmoke(HttpCase):\n    def test_tournaments_list_returns_200(self):\n        response = self.url_open(\"/tournaments\")\n        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)\n```\n\nHTTP tests are significantly slower than `TransactionCase` tests and require\n`sports_federation_portal` to be installed. Use the\n`unittest.SkipTest` pattern to skip gracefully when optional modules are absent:\n\n```python\n@classmethod\ndef setUpClass(cls):\n    super().setUpClass()\n    with cls.registry.cursor() as cr:\n        env = api.Environment(cr, SUPERUSER_ID, {})\n        if not env.ref(\"sports_federation_portal.group_federation_portal_club\",\n                       raise_if_not_found=False):\n            raise unittest.SkipTest(\"sports_federation_portal not installed\")\n```\n\n### `TransactionCase` with a mock `request` — controller unit tests\n\nUsed for: testing controller methods that call `request.env` without running an\nHTTP server. Inject a `SimpleNamespace` as the request stub and patch the\n`request` module attribute.\n\n```python\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nclass TestCompetitionsApiJson(TransactionCase):\n\n    def test_rate_limit_blocks_after_limit(self):\n        request_stub = SimpleNamespace(\n            env=self.env,\n            httprequest=SimpleNamespace(remote_addr=\"198.51.100.1\", headers={}),\n        )\n        controller = PublicTournamentHubController()\n        with patch(\n            \"odoo.addons.sports_federation_public_site\"\n            \".controllers.public_competitions.request\",\n            request_stub,\n        ):\n            result = controller.competitions_api_json()\n        self.assertIn(\"tournaments\", result)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Shared Fixture Patterns\n\n### Basic club/team/season fixture\n\nThis fixture appears in almost every module's test. Copy it as a starting point\nrather than re-inventing it.\n\n```python\n@classmethod\ndef setUpClass(cls):\n    super().setUpClass()\n    cls.club = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Fixture Club\", \"code\": \"FXC\",\n    })\n    cls.team_a = cls.env[\"federation.team\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Team A\", \"club_id\": cls.club.id, \"code\": \"TFA\",\n    })\n    cls.team_b = cls.env[\"federation.team\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Team B\", \"club_id\": cls.club.id, \"code\": \"TFB\",\n    })\n    cls.season = cls.env[\"federation.season\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Test Season\",\n        \"code\": \"TS26\",\n        \"date_start\": \"2026-01-01\",\n        \"date_end\": \"2026-12-31\",\n    })\n    cls.tournament = cls.env[\"federation.tournament\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Test Tournament\",\n        \"code\": \"TT26\",\n        \"season_id\": cls.season.id,\n        \"date_start\": \"2026-06-01\",\n        \"state\": \"open\",\n    })\n```\n\n### Portal ownership fixture\n\nWhen testing portal access, create users with the portal club group and link\nthem to a club via `federation.club.representative`. This pattern is used\nthroughout `sports_federation_portal/tests/`.\n\n```python\nfrom odoo import SUPERUSER_ID, api\nfrom odoo.tests.common import TransactionCase\n\nclass TestPortalOwnership(TransactionCase):\n\n    @classmethod\n    def setUpClass(cls):\n        super().setUpClass()\n        cls.portal_group = cls.env.ref(\n            \"sports_federation_portal.group_federation_portal_club\"\n        )\n        cls.club_a = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create(\n            {\"name\": \"Portal Club A\", \"code\": \"PCA\"}\n        )\n        cls.club_b = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create(\n            {\"name\": \"Portal Club B\", \"code\": \"PCB\"}\n        )\n\n        def _make_user(name, login):\n                cls.env[\"res.users\"]\n                .with_context(no_reset_password=True)\n                .create({\n                    \"login\": login,\n            )\n        cls.user_a = _make_user(\"User A\", \"usera@example.com\")\n\n\n## Maintained performance budgets\n\nThese are the current CI budgets for the most sensitive read paths:\n\n| Path | Budget |\n|---|---:|\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_featured_tournaments(limit=4)` | 1 query |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_live_tournaments(limit=4)` | 1 query |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_recent_result_tournaments(limit=4)` | 3 queries |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_schedule_sections()` | 4 queries |\n| `federation.team.roster.line._portal_get_available_players()` | 7 queries |\n| `federation.report.schedule._build_season_portfolio_rows()` | 3 queries |\n| `federation.report.schedule._build_club_performance_rows()` | 4 queries |\n| `federation.report.snapshot._compliance_pending_total()` | 1 aggregate query |\n| scheduling validation smoke | ≤ 25 seconds |\n\nThe authoritative tests are in the competition, public-site, portal,\nreporting, and domain test suites. Scheduling regressions belong in\n`sports_federation_scheduling/tests/` and calendar regressions belong in\n`sports_federation_calendar/tests/`.\nWhen a budget changes, update the assertion, explain the cause, and review the\ncorresponding SQL plan or index before changing the documented value.\n        # Link users to clubs via representative records\n        for user, club in ((cls.user_a, cls.club_a), (cls.user_b, cls.club_b)):\n            cls.env[\"federation.club.representative\"].create({\n                \"club_id\": club.id,\n                \"user_id\": user.id,\n                \"role_type_id\": cls.env.ref(\n                    \"sports_federation_portal.role_type_competition_contact\"\n                ).id,\n            })\n\n    def test_club_a_cannot_see_club_b_roster(self):\n        \"\"\"Portal user A must not be able to reach a roster belonging to club B.\"\"\"\n        privilege = self.env[\"federation.portal.privilege\"]\n        with self.assertRaises(AccessError):\n            privilege.with_user(self.user_a).portal_search_by_id(\n                \"federation.team.roster\",\n                self.club_b_roster.id,\n            )\n```\n\nSee `sports_federation_portal/tests/test_portal_ownership_guard.py` for a\ncomplete working example.\n\n### Frozen-time fixture for time-sensitive logic\n\nFor rate limiting, deadlines, and scheduling logic, freeze time with\n`unittest.mock.patch.object`:\n\n```python\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\ndef test_consume_blocks_after_limit(self):\n    service = self.env[\"federation.request.rate.limit\"].sudo()\n    frozen_time = datetime(2026, 6, 1, 12, 0, 0)\n    with patch.object(type(service), \"_get_now\", return_value=frozen_time):\n        first = service.consume(\"public_competitions_json\", \"ip:10.0.0.1\")\n        second = service.consume(\"public_competitions_json\", \"ip:10.0.0.1\")\n    self.assertTrue(first[\"allowed\"])\n    self.assertFalse(second[\"allowed\"])  # limit is 1 in this test\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Query-Count Budgets\n\nSeveral test suites assert exact query counts to catch N+1 regressions:\n\n```python\ndef test_public_discovery_helpers_stay_within_query_budget(self):\n    with self.assertQueryCount(1):\n        featured = self.env[\"federation.tournament\"].get_public_featured_tournaments(limit=4)\n    with self.assertQueryCount(3):\n        recent = self.env[\"federation.tournament\"].get_public_recent_result_tournaments(limit=4)\n```\n\n**Rules:**\n- `assertQueryCount(N)` fails if the block executes more or fewer than `N`\n  queries. Fewer queries is also a failure because it may indicate a test\n  regression (e.g., empty dataset masking missing queries).\n- Budgets live in the maintained performance section of this guide. If you\n    lower a budget, update that section.\n- If your change legitimately increases a query count, update the assertion\n    and add a comment explaining why, and update the budget table in this guide.\n\n---\n\n## 4. EXPLAIN Snapshot Tests\n\nFor critical query paths, the CI runs `ci/check_explain_snapshots.py`, which\ncompares `EXPLAIN` output against stored snapshots in `ci/explain_snapshots/`.\n\nTo add a new snapshot:\n\n```bash\n# Connect to the CI database and run EXPLAIN\ndocker compose exec db psql -U odoo -d odoo_ci_test -c \\\n  \"EXPLAIN SELECT ... FROM federation_tournament WHERE ...\"\n# Save the output to ci/explain_snapshots/<descriptive_name>.txt\n```\n\nA snapshot regression (e.g., a sequential scan replacing an index scan) fails\nCI. Review the output, add the necessary index, or update the snapshot with\na comment explaining the regression is acceptable.\n\n---\n\n## 5. What to Test for Each Change Type\n\n| Change type | Required test |\n|---|---|\n| New model field | `test_<model>_<field>_<rule>` validating the constraint or default |\n| New `@api.constrains` | One passing case + one failing case |\n| New computed field | Verify the value before and after the triggering field changes |\n| New wizard | End-to-end test: create input records → call wizard → assert output records (counts, states) |\n| New controller route | Either an HTTP smoke test (200/404) or a mock-request unit test |\n| Portal write | Always add a cross-club access-denial test in addition to the happy path |\n| Standings / schedule logic | `assertQueryCount` + correctness assertion for a minimal dataset |\n| Rate limiting | Frozen-time test: N requests pass, N+1 is blocked; verify `Retry-After` header |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Running Tests in CI\n\n```bash\n# Run the suite that covers your changed module\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\n\n# Run just one module (faster when iterating)\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_standings\n\n# Run participant readiness discovery guard (fails if post-tests are not discovered)\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite rosters_readiness_guard\n\n# Equivalent explicit form when you need direct control\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_rosters --test-tags sf_rosters_participant_readiness --require-post-tests 1\n\n# Skip browser bootstrap when the run has no HttpCase/browser coverage\nCI_SKIP_BROWSER_BOOTSTRAP=1 bash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\n\n# Frontend-only feedback loop: portal accessibility/mobile templates and\n# selected HTTP rendering tests\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --frontend-module sports_federation_portal\n\n# Detect changed addons and expand to all manifest reverse dependents\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --affected-from origin/main --include-dependents\n\n# Retain failed containers, database, filestore, generated config, and commands\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --module sports_federation_portal --keep-on-failure\n\n# Run all suites (used in final PR validation)\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh\n```\n\nThe CI runner produces a results block at the end:\n\n```\n\nEvery run also writes `test_failures.log`, `expected_diagnostics.log`,\n`infrastructure.log`, and `full.log` under `ci/logs/<timestamp>/`. A failed\n`--keep-on-failure` run writes `retained-container-commands.sh` with exact\ninspection and cleanup commands; successful runs clean up automatically.\n════════════════════════════════\n  RESULTS\n════════════════════════════════\n  Exit code:     0\n  Tests run:     62\n  Tests passed:  62\n  Tests failed:  0\n  Test errors:   0\n════════════════════════════════\n```\n\nA non-zero exit code means the CI gate failed. Check the container logs for\nthe failing test name and traceback:\n\n```bash\ndocker logs <container-name> 2>&1 | grep -A 20 \"FAIL\\|ERROR\"\n```\n\n### Discovery-safe tag convention\n\nFor high-risk regression suites, add an explicit class-level tag in addition to the usual install timing tags:\n\n```python\nfrom odoo.tests.common import TransactionCase, tagged\n\n@tagged(\"-at_install\", \"post_install\", \"sf_<module>_<feature>\")\nclass TestFeature(TransactionCase):\n    ...\n```\n\nUse lowercase snake-case tags prefixed with `sf_` (example: `sf_rosters_participant_readiness`).\nThis enables deterministic CI selection and allows `--require-post-tests` gating to detect discovery regressions early.\n\n### Critical suite registry\n\nThe CI runner enforces a minimum discovered post-test count for critical suites.\nThis prevents false-green runs where discovery is broken and no tests execute.\n\nGitHub Actions now also runs a dedicated integration matrix lane for\n`people_rosters_rules` and `ops_and_notifications` so cross-module behavior is\nvalidated on every push and pull request.\n\n| Suite | Default minimum post-tests | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `competition_core` | 1 | Core workflow sanity gate |\n| `portal_public_ops` | 1 | Portal/public ownership and route guard |\n| `finance_reporting` | 1 | Finance and reporting guard |\n| `release_surfaces` | 1 | Broad release surface guard |\n| `people_rosters_rules` | 1 | Domain rules and roster workflow guard |\n| `ops_and_notifications` | 1 | Operations and notification guard |\n| `rosters_readiness_guard` | 1 | Explicit tag `sf_rosters_participant_readiness` |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Test File Conventions\n\n- One test file per logical area in `tests/` (e.g., `test_round_robin.py`,\n  `test_knockout.py` — not one giant `test_all.py`).\n- File names: `test_<noun>.py` (noun = model name, workflow name, or feature).\n- Class name: `Test<Topic>` (e.g., `TestRoundRobin`, `TestPortalOwnershipGuard`).\n- `setUpClass` creates shared fixtures; individual test methods create only what\n  they uniquely need.\n- Test method names: `test_<what>_<expected_outcome>` (e.g.,\n  `test_duplicate_code_raises_validation_error`).\n- Register new test files in `tests/__init__.py` with `from . import test_myfile`.\n\n\n## Release performance qualification\n\nThe release-candidate pipeline runs the maintained query-budget lane after the core, portal, and public qualification lanes:\n\n```bash\nscripts/ci/run_rc_validation.sh performance\n```\n\nThe versioned registry is `ci/performance_budgets.json`. The contract checker verifies the exact targeted test classes, preventing a renamed class from silently turning the lane into an empty run. Budget increases require measured evidence in `RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md`. Synthetic data must be identified as synthetic.\n", "content_length": 15625, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "TESTING_GUIDE.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "beb29cbf3c47b4265e9292564d0718bd28acd72c87b8424d9761386429411eb6", "size_bytes": 15831}
+{"content": "# Testing Guide\n\nLast updated: 2026-05-18\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\n\nThis guide explains the project's test taxonomy, how to write tests for each\ncategory, and how to keep query-budget assertions current.\n\nPerformance budgets and explain snapshots are maintained here with the test\npatterns below. A query-count increase is a regression unless the test and the\nbudget are updated together with a reason.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Test Taxonomy\n\nThe project uses three test classes depending on what is being tested:\n\n### `TransactionCase` — ORM-level unit and integration tests\n\nUsed for: model methods, computed fields, `@api.constrains` validators,\nwizards, services, and controller methods that are tested by injecting a mock\n\nEach test runs inside a savepoint that is rolled back after the test, so data\n\nfrom odoo.tests.common import TransactionCase\n\n\n    @classmethod\n    def setUpClass(cls):\n        super().setUpClass()\n        cls.club = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\n            \"name\": \"Test Club\",\n            \"code\": \"TC01\",\n        })\n\n    def test_code_must_be_unique(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(Exception):\n            self.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\"name\": \"Dup\", \"code\": \"TC01\"})\n            self.env.cr.flush()\n```\n\n**Put shared fixtures in `setUpClass`.** Fixtures created there are shared\nacross all test methods (and rolled back as a unit at the end of the class).\nFixtures created inside individual test methods are rolled back after each\nmethod.\n### `HttpCase` — end-to-end HTTP smoke tests\n\nUsed for: verifying that routes return the expected HTTP status code, CSRF\ntoken handling, portal login flows.\n\nRequires a real database and a running Odoo HTTP server. These tests must be\ntagged `@tagged(\"-at_install\", \"post_install\")` so they run after all modules\nare loaded.\n\n```python\nfrom odoo.tests.common import HttpCase, tagged\n\n@tagged(\"-at_install\", \"post_install\")\nclass TestPublicSiteHttpSmoke(HttpCase):\n    def test_tournaments_list_returns_200(self):\n        response = self.url_open(\"/tournaments\")\n        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)\n```\n\nHTTP tests are significantly slower than `TransactionCase` tests and require\n`sports_federation_portal` to be installed. Use the\n`unittest.SkipTest` pattern to skip gracefully when optional modules are absent:\n\n```python\n@classmethod\ndef setUpClass(cls):\n    super().setUpClass()\n    with cls.registry.cursor() as cr:\n        env = api.Environment(cr, SUPERUSER_ID, {})\n        if not env.ref(\"sports_federation_portal.group_federation_portal_club\",\n                       raise_if_not_found=False):\n            raise unittest.SkipTest(\"sports_federation_portal not installed\")\n```\n\n### `TransactionCase` with a mock `request` — controller unit tests\n\nUsed for: testing controller methods that call `request.env` without running an\nHTTP server. Inject a `SimpleNamespace` as the request stub and patch the\n`request` module attribute.\n\n```python\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nclass TestCompetitionsApiJson(TransactionCase):\n\n    def test_rate_limit_blocks_after_limit(self):\n        request_stub = SimpleNamespace(\n            env=self.env,\n            httprequest=SimpleNamespace(remote_addr=\"198.51.100.1\", headers={}),\n        )\n        controller = PublicTournamentHubController()\n        with patch(\n            \"odoo.addons.sports_federation_public_site\"\n            \".controllers.public_competitions.request\",\n            request_stub,\n        ):\n            result = controller.competitions_api_json()\n        self.assertIn(\"tournaments\", result)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Shared Fixture Patterns\n\n### Basic club/team/season fixture\n\nThis fixture appears in almost every module's test. Copy it as a starting point\nrather than re-inventing it.\n\n```python\n@classmethod\ndef setUpClass(cls):\n    super().setUpClass()\n    cls.club = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Fixture Club\", \"code\": \"FXC\",\n    })\n    cls.team_a = cls.env[\"federation.team\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Team A\", \"club_id\": cls.club.id, \"code\": \"TFA\",\n    })\n    cls.team_b = cls.env[\"federation.team\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Team B\", \"club_id\": cls.club.id, \"code\": \"TFB\",\n    })\n    cls.season = cls.env[\"federation.season\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Test Season\",\n        \"code\": \"TS26\",\n        \"date_start\": \"2026-01-01\",\n        \"date_end\": \"2026-12-31\",\n    })\n    cls.tournament = cls.env[\"federation.tournament\"].create({\n        \"name\": \"Test Tournament\",\n        \"code\": \"TT26\",\n        \"season_id\": cls.season.id,\n        \"date_start\": \"2026-06-01\",\n        \"state\": \"open\",\n    })\n```\n\n### Portal ownership fixture\n\nWhen testing portal access, create users with the portal club group and link\nthem to a club via `federation.club.representative`. This pattern is used\nthroughout `sports_federation_portal/tests/`.\n\n```python\nfrom odoo import SUPERUSER_ID, api\nfrom odoo.tests.common import TransactionCase\n\nclass TestPortalOwnership(TransactionCase):\n\n    @classmethod\n    def setUpClass(cls):\n        super().setUpClass()\n        cls.portal_group = cls.env.ref(\n            \"sports_federation_portal.group_federation_portal_club\"\n        )\n        cls.club_a = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create(\n            {\"name\": \"Portal Club A\", \"code\": \"PCA\"}\n        )\n        cls.club_b = cls.env[\"federation.club\"].create(\n            {\"name\": \"Portal Club B\", \"code\": \"PCB\"}\n        )\n\n        def _make_user(name, login):\n                cls.env[\"res.users\"]\n                .with_context(no_reset_password=True)\n                .create({\n                    \"login\": login,\n            )\n        cls.user_a = _make_user(\"User A\", \"usera@example.com\")\n\n\n## Maintained performance budgets\n\nThese are the current CI budgets for the most sensitive read paths:\n\n| Path | Budget |\n|---|---:|\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_featured_tournaments(limit=4)` | 1 query |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_live_tournaments(limit=4)` | 1 query |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_recent_result_tournaments(limit=4)` | 3 queries |\n| `federation.tournament.get_public_schedule_sections()` | 4 queries |\n| `federation.team.roster.line._portal_get_available_players()` | 7 queries |\n| `federation.report.schedule._build_season_portfolio_rows()` | 3 queries |\n| `federation.report.schedule._build_club_performance_rows()` | 4 queries |\n| `federation.report.snapshot._compliance_pending_total()` | 1 aggregate query |\n| scheduling validation smoke | ≤ 25 seconds |\n\nThe authoritative tests are in the competition, public-site, portal,\nreporting, and domain test suites. Scheduling regressions belong in\n`sports_federation_scheduling/tests/` and calendar regressions belong in\n`sports_federation_calendar/tests/`.\nWhen a budget changes, update the assertion, explain the cause, and review the\ncorresponding SQL plan or index before changing the documented value.\n        # Link users to clubs via representative records\n        for user, club in ((cls.user_a, cls.club_a), (cls.user_b, cls.club_b)):\n            cls.env[\"federation.club.representative\"].create({\n                \"club_id\": club.id,\n                \"user_id\": user.id,\n                \"role_type_id\": cls.env.ref(\n                    \"sports_federation_portal.role_type_competition_contact\"\n                ).id,\n            })\n\n    def test_club_a_cannot_see_club_b_roster(self):\n        \"\"\"Portal user A must not be able to reach a roster belonging to club B.\"\"\"\n        privilege = self.env[\"federation.portal.privilege\"]\n        with self.assertRaises(AccessError):\n            privilege.with_user(self.user_a).portal_search_by_id(\n                \"federation.team.roster\",\n                self.club_b_roster.id,\n            )\n```\n\nSee `sports_federation_portal/tests/test_portal_ownership_guard.py` for a\ncomplete working example.\n\n### Frozen-time fixture for time-sensitive logic\n\nFor rate limiting, deadlines, and scheduling logic, freeze time with\n`unittest.mock.patch.object`:\n\n```python\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\ndef test_consume_blocks_after_limit(self):\n    service = self.env[\"federation.request.rate.limit\"].sudo()\n    frozen_time = datetime(2026, 6, 1, 12, 0, 0)\n    with patch.object(type(service), \"_get_now\", return_value=frozen_time):\n        first = service.consume(\"public_competitions_json\", \"ip:10.0.0.1\")\n        second = service.consume(\"public_competitions_json\", \"ip:10.0.0.1\")\n    self.assertTrue(first[\"allowed\"])\n    self.assertFalse(second[\"allowed\"])  # limit is 1 in this test\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Query-Count Budgets\n\nSeveral test suites assert exact query counts to catch N+1 regressions:\n\n```python\ndef test_public_discovery_helpers_stay_within_query_budget(self):\n    with self.assertQueryCount(1):\n        featured = self.env[\"federation.tournament\"].get_public_featured_tournaments(limit=4)\n    with self.assertQueryCount(3):\n        recent = self.env[\"federation.tournament\"].get_public_recent_result_tournaments(limit=4)\n```\n\n**Rules:**\n- `assertQueryCount(N)` fails if the block executes more or fewer than `N`\n  queries. Fewer queries is also a failure because it may indicate a test\n  regression (e.g., empty dataset masking missing queries).\n- Budgets live in the maintained performance section of this guide. If you\n    lower a budget, update that section.\n- If your change legitimately increases a query count, update the assertion\n    and add a comment explaining why, and update the budget table in this guide.\n\n---\n\n## 4. EXPLAIN Snapshot Tests\n\nFor critical query paths, the CI runs `ci/check_explain_snapshots.py`, which\ncompares `EXPLAIN` output against stored snapshots in `ci/explain_snapshots/`.\n\nTo add a new snapshot:\n\n```bash\n# Connect to the CI database and run EXPLAIN\ndocker compose exec db psql -U odoo -d odoo_ci_test -c \\\n  \"EXPLAIN SELECT ... FROM federation_tournament WHERE ...\"\n# Save the output to ci/explain_snapshots/<descriptive_name>.txt\n```\n\nA snapshot regression (e.g., a sequential scan replacing an index scan) fails\nCI. Review the output, add the necessary index, or update the snapshot with\na comment explaining the regression is acceptable.\n\n---\n\n## 5. What to Test for Each Change Type\n\n| Change type | Required test |\n|---|---|\n| New model field | `test_<model>_<field>_<rule>` validating the constraint or default |\n| New `@api.constrains` | One passing case + one failing case |\n| New computed field | Verify the value before and after the triggering field changes |\n| New wizard | End-to-end test: create input records → call wizard → assert output records (counts, states) |\n| New controller route | Either an HTTP smoke test (200/404) or a mock-request unit test |\n| Portal write | Always add a cross-club access-denial test in addition to the happy path |\n| Standings / schedule logic | `assertQueryCount` + correctness assertion for a minimal dataset |\n| Rate limiting | Frozen-time test: N requests pass, N+1 is blocked; verify `Retry-After` header |\n\n---\n\n## 6.  {"content": "# Troubleshooting Guide\n\nLast updated: 2026-05-18\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nReview cadence: Every release train\n\nThis guide documents known issues, their root causes, and the recommended\nrecovery steps. Always check this file before escalating to a developer.\n\n---\n\n## Quick Diagnostic Checklist\n\nBefore diving into a specific symptom, run these checks first:\n\n```bash\n# 1. All containers running?\ndocker compose ps\n\n# 2. Any Python errors in the Odoo log?\ndocker compose logs odoo 2>&1 | grep -i \"error\\|traceback\\|exception\" | tail -20\n\n# 3. PostgreSQL accepting connections?\ndocker compose exec db pg_isready -U odoo\n\n# 4. Scheduled actions healthy?\n# Check in the back office: Settings → Technical → Automation → Scheduled Actions\n# Look for any action where \"Last Execution\" shows a failure icon.\n```\n\n---\n\n## Symptom Index\n\n| Symptom | Section |\n|---|---|\n| Match form crashes with `UndefinedColumn` | [§1](#1-match-form-crashes-with-undefinedcolumn) |\n| Standings not recomputing after result approval | [§2](#2-standings-not-recomputing-after-result-approval) |\n| Notifications not sending | [§3](#3-notifications-not-sending) |\n| Import wizard dry-run always fails | [§4](#4-import-wizard-dry-run-always-fails) |\n| Rate limit buckets never cleared | [§5](#5-rate-limit-buckets-never-cleared) |\n| CI container fails to start PostgreSQL | [§6](#6-ci-container-fails-to-start-postgresql) |\n| Portal page shows 403 / `AccessError` | [§7](#7-portal-page-shows-403--accesserror) |\n| Public JSON feed returns `500` | [§8](#8-public-json-feed-returns-500) |\n| Player shows `is_eligible = False` after license activation | [§9](#9-player-shows-is_eligible--false-after-license-activation) |\n| Finance events not exporting to partner | [§10](#10-finance-events-not-exporting-to-partner) |\n| Reporting schedule stuck in `running` | [§11](#11-reporting-schedule-stuck-in-running) |\n| Roster assignment_ready False with no clear reason | [§12](#12-roster-assignment_ready-false-with-no-clear-reason) |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Match Form Crashes with `UndefinedColumn`\n\n**Symptom**: Opening or saving a match record raises a `ProgrammingError:\ncolumn \"xyz\" of relation \"federation_match\" does not exist`.\n\n**Cause**: A `store=True` computed field was added to the match model in a\nnew release but the database column was never created because the module was\nnot upgraded after the code change.\n\n**Fix**:\n\n```bash\n# Upgrade the affected module(s) to create the missing column\ndocker compose exec odoo odoo \\\n  -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf \\\n  -d odoo \\\n  -u sports_federation_tournament,sports_federation_result_control \\\n  --stop-after-init\ndocker compose restart odoo\n```\n\n**Prevention**: always run `upgrade_sports_federation.sh` (or `-u <module>`)\nafter pulling code that adds or changes stored computed fields. A missing\ncolumn never appears in `--test-enable` output but will crash the live form.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Standings Not Recomputing After Result Approval\n\n**Symptom**: A result is approved (`result_state = approved`,\n`include_in_official_standings = True`) but the standings table is unchanged.\n\n**Cause A**: The standings record for the affected tournament/stage/group is\nin `frozen` state. Frozen standings are never recomputed automatically.\n\n**Fix A**:\n\n1. Open **Federation → Standings** and locate the relevant standings record.\n2. Verify `state = frozen`.\n3. Click **Unfreeze** (requires Federation Manager group).\n4. Click **Recompute**.\n5. Verify the standings are correct, then re-freeze.\n\n**Cause B**: The `@api.depends` chain on the standings recompute trigger is\nmissing the field that changed.\n\n**Fix B**:\n\n1. Check which field on `federation.match` changed (e.g. `home_score`).\n2. Verify it appears in the `@api.depends` decorator on\n   `_trigger_recompute` or the equivalent compute method in\n   `sports_federation_standings/models/federation_standings.py`.\n3. If missing, add the field to the depends list and upgrade the module.\n\n**Cause C**: The automatic trigger code path was skipped because the result\nwas approved via a raw ORM write (`record.write({\"result_state\": \"approved\"})`)\ninstead of the `action_approve_result()` method. This can happen during\nbulk imports.\n\n**Fix C**: Trigger a manual recompute from the standings form.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Notifications Not Sending\n\n**Symptom**: Expected emails (result approval, roster confirmation, referee\nassignment) are not delivered.\n\n**Cause A**: SMTP server is not configured or is misconfigured.\n\n**Fix A**:\n1. Open **Settings → Technical → Email → Outgoing Mail Servers**.\n2. Verify the server entry exists and is active.\n3. Click **Test Connection**. If it fails, correct the host, port, and\n   credentials.\n4. Check `docker compose logs odoo | grep -i smtp` for connection errors.\n\n**Cause B**: `sports_federation_notifications` module is not installed.\n\n**Fix B**:\n```bash\ndocker compose exec odoo odoo -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf -d odoo \\\n  -i sports_federation_notifications --stop-after-init\n```\n\n**Cause C**: The notification dispatcher scheduled action is disabled.\n\n**Fix C**:\n1. Open **Settings → Technical → Automation → Scheduled Actions**.\n2. Find the federation notification scan action.\n3. Click **Run Manually** to test, then re-enable if it was disabled.\n\n**Cause D**: The email ends up in the mail queue but is not sent because the\nqueue runner is not processing.\n\n**Fix D**:\n```bash\n# Force the mail queue to flush\ndocker compose exec odoo odoo \\\n  -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf -d odoo \\\n  --load=base,mail --stop-after-init \\\n  -e \"self.env['mail.mail'].process_email_queue()\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Import Wizard Dry-Run Always Fails\n\n**Symptom**: Running a dry-run in **Federation → Import Tools → Import Wizard**\nraises a duplicate key or unique constraint error on every attempt, even for\na fresh payload.\n\n**Cause**: Stale rows in the staging table (`federation.import.staged.delivery`\nor the module-specific staging model) from a previous failed import still\noccupy the unique key slot.\n\n**Fix**:\n1. Open **Federation → Import Tools → Inbound Deliveries**.\n2. Filter for `state = staged` deliveries from the failing batch.\n3. Archive or delete the stale staged rows.\n\nAlternatively, trigger the cleanup scheduled action:\n1. Open **Settings → Technical → Automation → Scheduled Actions**.\n2. Run **Federation: GC Staged Deliveries** manually.\n\n**Prevention**: the GC scheduled action runs daily. If it is disabled (see §5),\nstale rows accumulate quickly.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Rate Limit Buckets Never Cleared\n\n**Symptom**: The `federation.request.rate.limit` table grows unboundedly.\nPublic API calls succeed less frequently than expected even from legitimate\ncallers whose rate-limit window has expired.\n\n**Cause**: The `Federation: GC Rate Limit Buckets` scheduled action is\ndisabled.\n\n**Fix**:\n1. Open **Settings → Technical → Automation → Scheduled Actions**.\n2. Find **Federation: GC Rate Limit Buckets**.\n3. Set **Active** = True and **Interval** to 1 Hour (the default).\n4. Click **Run Manually** to clear the backlog immediately.\n\n**Verify** bucket cleanup is working:\n```bash\ndocker compose exec -T db psql -U odoo -d odoo \\\n  -c \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM federation_request_rate_limit;\"\n# Should decrease after the GC action runs\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. CI Container Fails to Start PostgreSQL\n\n**Symptom**: Running `bash addons/ci/run_tests.sh` fails immediately with\na PostgreSQL startup error: `address already in use` or `port 5432 already\nin use`.\n\n**Cause**: The development Docker Compose stack is already running and its\n`db` service occupies port 5432 (if the CI uses host networking) or the same\nDocker network.\n\n**Fix**:\n\n```bash\n# Option A: stop the dev stack before running CI\ndocker compose down\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core\n\n# Option B: use a different network (check ci/run_tests.sh --help for options)\nbash addons/ci/run_tests.sh --suite competition_core --network ci_net\n```\n\nAfter CI completes, restart the dev stack:\n```bash\ndocker compose up -d\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. Portal Page Shows 403 / `AccessError`\n\n**Symptom**: A portal user navigates to a federation portal page and receives\na 403 error or an `AccessError` in the logs.\n\n**Cause A**: The user's `res.users` record is not linked to a\n`federation.club.representative` record for the club that owns the resource.\n\n**Fix A**:\n1. Open **Federation → Portal → Club Representatives**.\n2. Verify a record exists for the user and the correct club.\n3. If missing, create it (see [WORKFLOW_CLUB_ONBOARDING.md](\n   _workflows/WORKFLOW_CLUB_ONBOARDING.md)).\n\n**Cause B**: The portal record rule for the affected model uses the wrong\ndomain expression (developer error introduced by a recent change).\n\n**Fix B**:\n1. Check **Settings → Technical → Security → Record Rules** for rules on the\n   affected model.\n2. Verify the domain matches the expected club-scoping pattern\n   (`club_id.representative_ids.user_id`, etc.).\n\n---\n\n## 8. Public JSON Feed Returns `500`\n\n**Symptom**: `GET /competitions/api/json` returns HTTP 500 instead of JSON.\n\n**Cause A**: A new required computed field on `federation.tournament` throws\nan exception when the tournament has incomplete data.\n\n**Fix A**: Check the Odoo log for the traceback:\n```bash\ndocker compose logs odoo 2>&1 | grep -A 30 \"competitions/api/json\"\n```\nFix the underlying data or guard the computed field with a `try/except`.\n\n**Cause B**: The rate-limit table has a corrupt bucket (e.g. `hit_count < 0`).\n\n**Fix B**:\n```bash\ndocker compose exec -T db psql -U odoo -d odoo \\\n  -c \"DELETE FROM federation_request_rate_limit WHERE hit_count < 0;\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 9. Player Shows `is_eligible = False` After License Activation\n\n**Symptom**: A license is set to `state = active` but the player's\n`is_eligible` computed field remains `False`.\n\n**Cause A**: Odoo's compute cache is stale. This can happen after a direct\ndatabase write (e.g. during a migration or import) that bypasses the ORM.\n\n**Fix A**:\n```bash\n# Force recompute of is_eligible for all players\ndocker compose exec odoo odoo shell -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf -d odoo <<'EOF'\nenv['federation.player'].search([])._compute_is_eligible()\nenv.cr.commit()\nEOF\n```\n\n**Cause B**: The player also has an active suspension sanction in\n`sports_federation_discipline`. Suspension overrides the license-active state.\n\n**Fix B**: Check **Federation → Discipline → Sanctions** for an active\nsuspension for the player. If the suspension is resolved, run the\nreinstatement action (see [WORKFLOW_PLAYER_LICENSE.md](\n_workflows/WORKFLOW_PLAYER_LICENSE.md)).\n\n---\n\n## 10. Finance Events Not Exporting to Partner\n\n**Symptom**: A partner calls `GET /integration/v1/finance-events` and\nreceives an empty list even though events exist in the system.\n\n**Cause A**: Events are still in `draft` state. The integration export endpoint\nreturns only `confirmed` or `exported` events.\n\n**Fix A**: Federation finance staff must confirm the pending events:\n**Federation → Finance → Finance Events → filter `state = draft` → Confirm**.\n\n**Cause B**: The partner's `X-Federation-Partner-Code` header does not match\nthe code stored on the `federation.integration.partner` record.\n\n**Fix B**: Verify the partner code with the administrator. The export route\nreturns `access_denied` (not an empty list) for authentication failures.\nIf the response is an empty list with status 200, the authentication succeeded\nbut no confirmed events match the filter.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Reporting Schedule Stuck in `running`\n\n**Symptom**: A report schedule shows `state = running` indefinitely; no report\nis generated and no error is visible.\n\n**Cause**: The Odoo worker that started the report generation crashed (e.g.\nOOM kill, container restart) mid-execution, leaving the state flag uncleared.\n\n**Fix**:\n1. Open **Federation → Reporting → Report Schedules**.\n2. Set the stuck schedule back to `draft` or `ready` via the developer mode\n   form editor, or use:\n\n```bash\ndocker compose exec odoo odoo shell -c /etc/odoo/odoo.conf -d odoo <<'EOF'\nenv['federation.report.schedule'].search([('state','=','running')])\\\n  .write({'state': 'ready'})\nenv.cr.commit()\nEOF\n```\n\n3. Trigger the schedule manually to confirm it completes.\n\n**Prevention**: monitor the **Reporting → Report Schedules** list after every\ncontainer restart.\n\n---\n\n## 12. Roster `assignment_ready` False with No Clear Reason\n\n**Symptom**: A roster line shows `assignment_ready = False` but the\n`readiness_feedback` field is empty or unhelpful.\n\n**Cause**: The eligibility service (`federation.eligibility.service`) failed\nsilently or the `readiness_feedback` compute method has a gap for a new\nblocking condition that was added without a corresponding feedback string.\n\n**Fix**:\n1. Open the roster line form and check the **Readiness** tab.\n2. If feedback is empty, check the player's license state, suspension status,\n   and team/season scope manually.\n3. If this is a new condition that lacks feedback, add the missing feedback\n   string in `sports_federation_rosters/models/roster_line.py` →\n   `_compute_readiness_feedback`.\n\n---\n\n## Getting Further Help\n\nIf none of the above applies:\n\n1. Capture the full traceback from the Odoo log:\n   ```bash\n   docker compose logs odoo 2>&1 | grep -A 50 \"Traceback\"\n   ```\n2. Note the affected model, user, and action (what was clicked or called).\n3. Check `CONTRIBUTING.md` for how to run focused module tests that may\n   reproduce the issue.\n4. Open a bug report in the project tracker with the log excerpt, module\n   version (`__manifest__.py` `version` field), and reproduction steps.\n", "content_length": 13484, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "TROUBLESHOOTING.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "263f32077dd46a75aec4d6aee173880e9efcc6ccb8bf2d2f91744ed4f61753d1", "size_bytes": 13567}
 {"content": "# Workflow: Club Representative Appeal & Dispute\n\nEnd-to-end process for raising, reviewing, and resolving a result or standing\ndispute submitted by a club representative.\n\nThis workflow is intentionally a thin, requester-facing wrapper around\n`WORKFLOW_GOVERNANCE_OVERRIDE.md`, which is the canonical exception engine.\nDo not duplicate full exception mechanics here; use this workflow to describe\nclub-entry and follow-up touchpoints only.\n\n## Overview\n\nWhen a club representative believes a match result is incorrect or that a ruling\nwas applied unfairly, they can raise a formal dispute. The dispute is captured as\na `federation.override_request` and goes through the governance review pipeline.\nThe outcome may be a result correction, a sanction reduction, a standing\nadjustment, or a formal rejection with reasons.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_governance` | Override requests, decisions, audit notes, outcomes |\n| `sports_federation_result_control` | Result correction after approved override |\n| `sports_federation_standings` | Standing adjustment after result correction |\n| `sports_federation_portal` | Club representative raises appeal via portal or back-office |\n| `sports_federation_notifications` | Notifications to all parties at key decision points |\n| `sports_federation_base` | Core club / match records referenced by the request |\n| `mail` | Chatter audit trail on the override request |\n\n## Models\n\n| Model | Key Fields |\n|-------|-----------|\n| `federation.override.request` | `title`, `request_type`, `target_model`, `target_res_id`, `reason`, `state`, `requester_id`, `decision_note`, `implementation_note` |\n\n## Override Request Types relevant to appeals\n\n| Type | Use case |\n|------|---------|\n| `result_correction` | Incorrect score, forfeit dispute, missing goal |\n| `standing_adjustment` | Points awarded incorrectly; does not require score change |\n| `eligibility_waiver` | Player ruled ineligible unfairly |\n| `sanction_reduction` | Disciplinary fine or suspension reduction request |\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\ndraft → submitted → approved → implemented → closed\n                 ↘ rejected  → closed\n      ← withdrawn (submitted → draft)\n```\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Club Representative Raises a Dispute\n\n**Actor**: Club representative\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance` (via portal or back office)\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Governance → Override Requests** (back office) or\n   the portal appeal section (if enabled).\n2. Click **New** and fill in:\n   - **Title** — clear one-line description of the dispute\n   - **Request Type** — e.g. `result_correction`\n   - **Target Record** — the match, result, or standing being disputed (set\n     `target_model` and `target_res_id`)\n   - **Reason** — full factual justification\n3. Save in `draft` state. Add supporting attachments via the chatter.\n4. Click **Submit** when ready: `draft → submitted`.\n\nThe requester and request timestamp are recorded automatically.\n\n**Withdrawal**: if the club needs to amend the request before a decision is\nmade, click **Withdraw** to return from `submitted → draft`, edit, and resubmit.\n\n### 2. Federation Staff Governance Review\n\n**Actor**: Governance officer\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. Open the submitted request from\n   **Federation → Governance → Override Requests → Submitted**.\n2. Review the request, target record, and any supporting attachments.\n3. Use the chatter to request clarification from the club if needed (keeps a\n   timestamped record).\n4. Add **Audit Notes** for internal commentary that should not be visible to\n   the club representative.\n\nThere is no separate `under_review` state. Operational review happens while the\nrequest remains in `submitted` state.\n\n### 3. Decision Recording\n\n**Actor**: Governance officer\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\nAfter review, the governance officer decides:\n\n- **Approve**: `submitted → approved`\n  - Add a `decision_note` explaining the basis for approval.\n  - The standard approve action creates a decision record automatically.\n- **Reject**: `submitted → rejected`\n  - Add a `decision_note` with the rejection reason.\n  - The club representative is notified (if notifications module is installed).\n\nThe governance officer records only the decision — implementation is handled\nseparately to keep the audit trail clean.\n\n### 4. Decision Notification\n\n**Actor**: System\n**Module**: `sports_federation_notifications`\n\nOn state transition to `approved` or `rejected`:\n\n- The club representative (requester) receives a notification with the decision\n  and the `decision_note`.\n- The federation manager group receives a notification for `approved` requests\n  that require implementation.\n\n### 5. Outcome Implementation\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance` + affected owning module\n\nIf approved:\n\n| Request Type | Implementation Action |\n|---|---|\n| `result_correction` | Update `home_score` / `away_score` on the match; re-run result approval pipeline |\n| `standing_adjustment` | Unfreeze standings if frozen; manually adjust; re-freeze |\n| `eligibility_waiver` | Create or reinstate player license; update roster line |\n| `sanction_reduction` | Edit disciplinary sanction amount or suspension length |\n\nSteps:\n1. Open the approved override request.\n2. Implement the correction in the relevant module (see table above).\n3. Record what was done in `implementation_note` on the request form.\n4. Click **Mark Implemented**: `approved → implemented`.\n5. An outcome-log row is written automatically so later review can see what was\n   done, not just what was approved.\n\n### 6. Closure\n\n**Actor**: Governance officer or federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\nOnce all follow-up is complete:\n\n1. Open the `implemented` or `rejected` request.\n2. Click **Close**: state → `closed`.\n3. Only `implemented` or `rejected` requests can be closed.\n4. Closed requests are read-only and permanently preserved in the audit trail.\n\n## Audit Trail\n\nThe complete audit trail includes:\n\n- Request creation with requester and timestamp\n- Submission and any withdrawal back to draft (with timestamps)\n- Decision (approve / reject) with decision note and officer identity\n- Implementation note describing what was done\n- Closure timestamp\n\nAll state transitions are logged in the chatter via `mail.tracking.value`.\n\n## Impact on Standings\n\nFor result corrections that change scores:\n\n1. Corrected result re-enters the approval pipeline: the administrator re-submits\n   and re-approves the result.\n2. Standings in `draft` or `ready` state recompute automatically.\n3. If standings are `frozen`, the administrator must unfreeze → recompute →\n   re-freeze (see [WORKFLOW_STANDINGS_LIFECYCLE.md](WORKFLOW_STANDINGS_LIFECYCLE.md)).\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [WORKFLOW_GOVERNANCE_OVERRIDE.md](WORKFLOW_GOVERNANCE_OVERRIDE.md) — Generic override request process for all exception types.\n- [WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md](WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md) — Result approval and re-approval after correction.\n- [WORKFLOW_STANDINGS_LIFECYCLE.md](WORKFLOW_STANDINGS_LIFECYCLE.md) — Freeze / unfreeze and recompute steps.\n\nUnified operator model reminder:\n\n- Raise dispute/request exception on the owning record.\n- Resolve through Governance Override.\n- Re-run result approval and standings publication recovery where needed.\n", "content_length": 7409, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_APPEAL_DISPUTE.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "063f3e885e799adca4fdff955d7222fbc4332d02038b775bc52176ca5601c251", "size_bytes": 7467}
 {"content": "# Workflow: Club Onboarding\n\nEnd-to-end process for registering a new club with the federation — from initial\nrequest through compliance document intake, bank-details entry, first-season\nregistration, and portal access grant.\n\n## Overview\n\nA new club must be formally onboarded before it can enter teams in tournaments.\nOnboarding spans four areas: master-data setup, compliance document collection,\nfinance details, and portal access for the club representative. These steps can\nrun partly in parallel once the club record exists.\n\nUse this workflow as the primary new-club journey. Compliance, finance, and\nseason-registration workflows support it, but they should not be presented as\nseparate equal-weight starting points when staff are onboarding a brand-new\nclub.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_base` | Club, team, and season master data |\n| `sports_federation_compliance` | Document requirement tracking |\n| `sports_federation_portal` | Club representative portal access |\n| `sports_federation_finance_bridge` | Bank details, registration fee event |\n| `sports_federation_notifications` | Welcome and missing-document notifications |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. New Club Request\n\n**Actor**: Club applicant (off-system) → Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_base`\n\nThe federation receives a new-club application through its standard intake\nchannel (email, paper form, or future portal form). The federation administrator\ncreates the club record:\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Configuration → Clubs** and click **New**.\n2. Fill in:\n   - **Name** — official club name\n   - **Code** — short unique code (e.g. `MFC`)\n   - **Email**, **Phone**, **Website** (optional)\n   - **Address** — postal address for correspondence\n3. Save. The club starts with `active = True` and no additional state.\n\nAt this point the club exists in the system but is not yet enrolled in any season\nand has no compliance documents or portal access.\n\n### 2. Federation Staff Review\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_base` + `sports_federation_compliance`\n\nBefore proceeding with compliance intake, the administrator performs a background\nreview:\n\n- Verify the club is not a duplicate (search by name and code).\n- Confirm the founding documents meet federation bylaws.\n- Confirm at least one responsible contact person has been nominated.\n\nNo formal review state exists on the club record itself. Use the chatter\n(Discuss tab on the club form) to record review notes.\n\n### 3. Compliance Document Intake\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator or club contact\n**Module**: `sports_federation_compliance`\n\nCreate one `federation.compliance.submission` per document requirement that\napplies to clubs (`required_for_all = True` for the `club` target model):\n\nCommon requirements at onboarding:\n\n| Requirement | Expiry? | Notes |\n|------------|---------|-------|\n| Public Liability Insurance | Yes (annual) | Minimum coverage amount per federation rules |\n| Club Registration Certificate | No | Official registration with national authority |\n| Bank Account Confirmation Letter | No | Matches bank details to be entered |\n| Club Rules & Constitution | No | Signed copy |\n\nFor each document:\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Compliance → Document Submissions**.\n2. Create a submission linked to the club and the relevant requirement.\n3. Attach the scanned or PDF document.\n4. Submit: state `draft → submitted`.\n5. The compliance officer reviews and approves: state `submitted → approved`.\n\nIf `sports_federation_notifications` is installed, an approval confirmation is\nsent to the club contact on each approval.\n\n### 4. Bank Details Entry\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. On the club form, open the **Finance** tab (added by `sports_federation_finance_bridge`).\n2. Enter bank account details (IBAN, bank name, account holder name).\n3. These details are used when generating registration-fee finance events for\n   accounting integration exports.\n\n### 5. First Season Registration\n\n**Actor**: Club administrator or club representative\n**Module**: `sports_federation_base` + `sports_federation_portal`\n\nOnce compliance documents are approved, the club can register for the current\nseason:\n\n**Via back-office**:\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Registrations → Season Registrations**.\n2. Create a registration record for the club's first team in the current season.\n3. State: `draft → submitted → confirmed`.\n\n**Via portal (if portal module is installed)**:\n\n1. Club representative logs into the portal.\n2. Navigates to the Season Registrations section.\n3. Submits a registration request (creates in `submitted` state).\n4. Federation administrator confirms from the back office.\n\nA registration-fee finance event is created automatically on confirmation.\n\n### 6. Club Representative Portal Access Grant\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_portal`\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Portal → Club Representatives**.\n2. Create a `federation.club.representative` record:\n   - **Club** — the newly onboarded club\n   - **User** — invite the contact as an Odoo portal user (`res.users` with\n     the portal group), or select an existing portal user\n   - **Role Type** — select the appropriate role (e.g. `Competition Contact`,\n     `Finance Contact`)\n3. Save.\n4. The representative is now linked to the club. Portal record rules limit them\n   to records belonging to their club.\n\n**Sending the portal invitation**: use the standard Odoo **Grant portal access**\nwizard from the contact (res.partner) record associated with the representative's\nuser. This sends the portal invitation email.\n\nOnce the invitation is accepted, the representative can:\n\n- Submit season registration requests\n- Upload compliance documents\n- View their club's roster and match schedule\n- Submit match result sheets\n\n## Checklist\n\n| Step | Owner | Required before |\n|------|-------|----------------|\n| Club record created | Federation admin | All other steps |\n| Compliance docs approved | Compliance officer | First tournament entry |\n| Bank details entered | Federation admin | Finance event export |\n| Season registration confirmed | Federation admin | Tournament participation |\n| Portal user invited | Federation admin | Club representative self-service |\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [WORKFLOW_SEASON_REGISTRATION.md](WORKFLOW_SEASON_REGISTRATION.md) — Full season-registration process including team enrolment and player licensing.\n- [WORKFLOW_COMPLIANCE_MANAGEMENT.md](WORKFLOW_COMPLIANCE_MANAGEMENT.md) — Document submission and review details.\n- [WORKFLOW_FINANCIAL_TRACKING.md](WORKFLOW_FINANCIAL_TRACKING.md) — Registration fee event creation.\n", "content_length": 6765, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_CLUB_ONBOARDING.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "f90594ca00eef47ff06760d60a110814a84cb13c23449694cf968717bc93eba1", "size_bytes": 6811}

+{"content": "# Delivery Roadmap\n\nOwner: Federation Platform Team\nLast updated: 2026-09-02\nReview cadence: Every release\n\n## Purpose\n\nThis roadmap turns the stabilized competition architecture into an executable\nsequence of release, product, reliability, operations, and usability milestones.\nThe ownership chain remains unchanged:\n\n`registration → format → calendar → scheduling → schedule approval → match day`\n\nPublished schedules remain immutable, role separation remains enforced, and\nportal/public access continues through explicit ownership boundaries.\n\n## Release Baseline Qualification\n\nStatus: **Implemented, pending execution on the committed candidate**\n\n### Objective\n\nCreate one reproducible candidate commit for which static validation, fresh\ninstallation, focused release surfaces, performance qualification, browser\nfocus tests, and the full standard suite all pass.\n\n### Automation\n\nRun after committing all functional changes:\n\n```bash\nscripts/ci/run_release_baseline.sh\n```\n\nThe script executes:\n\n1. clean-workspace preflight;\n2. static and contract validation;\n3. fresh installation and standard tests;\n4. core qualification;\n5. portal qualification;\n6. public-site qualification;\n7. performance qualification;\n8. browser and release-focus qualification;\n9. final full standard suite;\n10. machine-readable evidence capture per lane.\n\nEvidence is written under `artifacts/release/baseline/` by default. Override this\nwith `RELEASE_BASELINE_EVIDENCE_DIR` when CI provides a dedicated artifact\ndirectory.\n\n### Exit criteria\n\n- One immutable candidate SHA is recorded in every evidence file.\n- Test failures and errors are zero.\n- Static, ownership, workflow, migration, publication, and security contracts pass.\n- Performance budgets pass.\n- Browser lifecycle, finance, and public-site tours pass.\n- Local and GitHub release-candidate validation agree on the same commit.\n- The worktree is clean before qualification starts.\n\n## Release Candidate and Migration Evidence\n\nStatus: **Next**\n\n### Objective\n\nProve deployment and recovery against a representative pre-refactor database and\nmatching filestore.\n\n### Deliverables\n\n- production-like database and filestore restore;\n- pre-upgrade invariant snapshot;\n- reviewed module upgrade;\n- post-upgrade invariant comparison;\n- role-separated operator pilot;\n- backup checksum and restore evidence;\n- rollback drill with named owner and trigger;\n- archived RC logs and machine-readable evidence.\n\n### Exit criteria\n\nThe upgraded copy preserves competition, registration, fixture, schedule,\npublication, match-day, result, standings, portal, public, integration, and\nattachment invariants. Restore and rollback complete from the recorded backup.\n\n## Simplified Competition Journey\n\nStatus: **Planned**\n\n### Objective\n\nAllow a normal league, tournament day, or cup to be configured, scheduled,\napproved, and published without exposing the underlying Odoo model structure.\n\n### Deliverables\n\n- template-led competition creation;\n- guided Basics, Participants, Format, Calendar, Schedule, Publish journey;\n- user-facing Draft, Ready, Live, Finished presentation states;\n- visible blockers, responsible role, and next action;\n- advanced stage graph, revision, fairness, and shared-day controls separated\n  from the normal path;\n- task-first club portal.\n\n### Exit criteria\n\nA trained non-developer completes the normal journey without developer mode,\ndirect model navigation, manual progression mapping, or shell access.\n\n## Operational Job Reliability\n\nStatus: **Partially implemented**\n\n### Objective\n\nGeneralize the proven standings recompute reliability pattern without moving\ndomain execution out of its owning addon.\n\n### Deliverables\n\n- bounded retry and backoff;\n- stale-running recovery;\n- dead-letter/operator-action state;\n- correlation IDs and sanitized error evidence;\n- idempotent manual retry;\n- operator visibility for notification, report, integration, retention, and\n  snapshot jobs;\n- shared infrastructure only after two proven consumers exist.\n\n### Exit criteria\n\nOperators can identify what failed, whether it will retry, when it will retry,\nhow many attempts remain, which correlation ID applies, and whether manual retry\nis safe.\n\n## Retention Evidence and Recovery Visibility\n\nStatus: **Partially implemented**\n\n### Objective\n\nTurn retention execution into auditable operational evidence ID;\n- read-only retention evidence views;\n- overdue and repeated-failure alerts;\n- release-readiness checks for disabled or stale retention jobs;\n- links to restore and rollback evidence.\n\n### Exit criteria\n\nThe release owner can prove that each policy ran, used the intended window,\nretained unresolved workflow evidence, cleaned matching attachments, and exposed\nfailures to Create a season and competition edition.\n2. Open registration and finalize the participant set.\n3. Freeze the competition structure and generate fixtures.\n4. Prepare match-day capacity and assign every fixture to a slot.\n5. Submit the schedule for independent review and publish the approved revision.\n6. Assign named officials or a club officiating duty and resolve shortages.\n7. Open match-day control, record results, and approve official results.\n8. Recompute and publish standings.\n9. Confirm the public competition, schedule, format, and standings pages.\n10. Confirm finance-event creation, settlement, export readiness, and traceability.\n11. Amend one published schedule through a replacement revision and verify that\n    the original publication and audit evidence remain immutable.\n12.  {"content": "# Competition Workflow Ownership\n\nThe ownership chain is the supported competition-planning architecture. No\nproduction data migration is required solely for this ownership clarification.\n\n## Capability ownership\n\n- Competition Core owns edition lifecycle and role assignments.\n- Registration owns entries and versioned finalized participant sets.\n- Format Studio owns stage topology, logical fixtures, standings snapshots, and progression.\n- Calendar owns physical match days, courts, and availability slots.\n- Scheduling owns fixture-to-slot assignments and fairness proposals.\n- Schedule Approval owns independent review and immutable publications.\n- Result Control owns the complete result lifecycle.\n- Match-Day Operations owns live execution and incidents.\n\n## Domain event contract\n\nEvents are past-tense facts. Producers emit only after a successful state change. Consumers must be idempotent and may not mutate the producer aggregate. Event payloads contain stable record IDs, version/revision, actor, and reason where applicable.\n\nRequired events: participant_set_finalized, structure_frozen, stage_standings_frozen, stage_progression_applied, matchday_capacity_ready, schedule_submitted, schedule_approved, schedule_published, matchday_opened, result_approved, result_contested, result_corrected, matchday_closed.\n", "content_length": 1325, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "docs/competition_engine_ownership.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "3352ecae7e79ab9a7fa5079eca2e62c212b65a59136effde4e6945b21563894c", "size_bytes": 1325}
 {"content": "# Exception Handling Policy\n\nCatch expected domain failures narrowly (`ValidationError`, `AccessError`, `UserError`). Integration boundaries may catch `Exception` only when they log with correlation context, classify the failure, persist operator feedback, and do not report success. Silent `except Exception: pass` is prohibited. Cron batches isolate individual records but retain error evidence. Authorization errors must never be downgraded to not-found or success responses.\n", "content_length": 479, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "docs/exception_handling_policy.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "623ac950360ff1053afe9defae8055d203f162ee48ffc995b8c24016637e1cfe", "size_bytes": 479}
